# Pantopus next steps

The founder's single checklist. Check items off as they ship. Started September 16, 2026; rewritten October 3, 2026 for the mobile pilot. The street and social designs and PR 625 were folded in the same day.

**Agents: build from the [mobile pilot build brief](docs/mobile-pilot-build-brief-2026-10-03.md).** It says exactly what to build, on which platforms, against which code, in what order, and what not to build. This file tracks status and decisions; the brief holds the instructions.

Sources, in order of authority for this work:

1. [Mobile pilot build brief](docs/mobile-pilot-build-brief-2026-10-03.md): what to build now, on iOS and Android, plus the backend they need.
2. The September 26 launch direction and its amendments, in `docs/launch-boundary-2026-09-26/` (merged from [PR 1499](https://github.com/WangPantopus/skinny-pantopus/pull/1499) on October 5). The brief carries every part this build needs.
3. [First-person loop design](docs/first-person-loop-design-2026-09-16.md): the full F1 to F12 designs. The brief says which parts are in.
4. Claude Design exports in `docs/design/exports/`, checked in `docs/design/exports/VERIFICATION.md`: the look of the screens that have one.
5. Background: the [design docs review](https://claude.ai/code/artifact/09276a14-1587-41be-86ef-54e95505d2d8), the [idea ledger](https://claude.ai/code/artifact/51f601e7-1e56-416e-b549-fbd036e421ba) and the [Wedge v2 strategy](https://claude.ai/artifact/GLUkeCRhnFZnsj7tRr1L4D).

**Designed, outside the mobile build.** The [Street Organizer](docs/product/street-organizer-design-2026-09-27.md) (Crew Day first), [Pulse](https://github.com/WangPantopus/skinny-pantopus/pull/625) (local conversation; see its [review comment](https://github.com/WangPantopus/skinny-pantopus/pull/625#issuecomment-5974324844)) and [Porchlight](docs/product/porchlight-product-design-2026-09-26.md) (check-ins for someone living alone). The [Pantopus Agent](docs/product/pantopus-agent-product-design-2026-10-04.md) ([system design](docs/product/pantopus-agent-system-design-2026-10-04.md)) is the one box that ties them together. Crew Day's first two phases run by hand beside the pilot (section 4A). The rest waits for evidence (sections 5 and 7).

**Who works on what (from October 6).** Four launch streams work in parallel. Each merges its own PRs after green CI and a check in the real apps; ground rules are in [AGENTS.md](AGENTS.md).

- **L1 Place, Today and reminders:** WP1 to WP5 and WP7 in section 3, the seeder, provider data and every reminder push.
- **L2 Home and household:** WP6, Add Home and verification, members, tasks, documents, guests and home privacy.
- **L3 Accounts, community, Mail, Support Trains and money:** WP8, sign-in, Nearby, chat, Mail, payments and the AI assistant.
- **L4 Release, production and quality:** hosted staging and production, store builds and listings, CI, security, performance and the final release-candidate sweep. L4 keeps this checklist current from the streams' PRs and prepares every founder step in section 2.

Sessions you start for one job (Instant Screens, Nearby Plans, single fixes) work beside them under the same merge rules.

## 0. Decisions

**Made September 16. All stand.**

- [x] Navigation stays Place · Today · Nearby · Mail; no relabel. (Changed October 13 for Pulse: five tabs, see below.)
- [x] Density meter: scarcity gates status only, never access.
- [x] Seeder stays as a visibly labeled platform publisher; strip the engagement-question and neighbor-voice prompt lines; exclude it from organic metrics.
- [x] General ban stays; a Moment category only with an explicit place attachment, and not before place pages exist.
- [x] No SavedItem / Action / Watch tables; extend `SavedPlace` and `HomeRecordWatch` instead.
- [x] Pilot is available nationwide and observed locally; paid spend only after week-four return is measured.
- [x] Cash Earn hidden until a balance is withdrawable.
- [x] Address-verified vs household-verified: only postcard, document, landlord or admin verification unlocks attested artifacts and neighbor messaging; an accepted household invite gives household tools only (design doc F3b). (Changed November 17: a fast check also opens messaging; attested artifacts still need the mailed code. See below.)

**Made September 27.**

- [x] Nine features leave the first launch behind launch flags, with their code kept ([launch-scope-flags-2026-10-01.md](docs/launch-scope-flags-2026-10-01.md)); Support Train gift funds became the ninth on October 6 (PR 1624).
- [x] Rebooking a known crew, the repeat Crew Day of the Street Organizer design, replaces the open gigs marketplace. Payments, tips, invoices and the scheduling engine stay.

**Made by the founder on October 3.**

- [x] New builds are mobile only: iOS and Android. Web waits. Backend, database and seeder changes the apps need are in scope and must not break the current web. **Exception, October 6 (founder): Ballot is built on the web as well; see the Ballot section.**
- [x] F1 to F12 for the pilot: build F1, F4 and the rest of F9's truthfulness fixes; build small versions of F2, F3 and F5; ship F3b's server gate with F3; leave F7, F8, F10 and F12's appeal windows for later; drop F6 and F11; park the stylized home picture and the address QR inbox. (Changed October 14: the home picture is built, see above.)

**Made on October 3 under the founder's standing instruction to go with the recommended option. Change any of them here.**

- [x] Pickup reminders push only for a pickup day the household confirmed. City defaults show as unconfirmed in the app and never push.
- [x] Pickup and radon reminders need a home. A private setup counts, so a household gets reminders the day it adds its home, without waiting for verification. A saved place without a home gets Today and an "Add your home" row where a reminder would be.
- [x] The radon card and the first-use card live on the Today tab, so they work for private-setup homes, which have no Place dashboard. Radon answers are stored as home tasks; "Not now" is remembered on the device.
- [x] The night-before pickup push rides the existing evening briefing. No new schedule.
- [x] Holiday moves come from city-level holiday rows that shift that week's pickups. No per-home edits and no new table.
- [x] F3b's server gate ships with the household step. Everyone verified by postcard, document, landlord or admin stays verified; people who joined by invitation become household-verified.
- [x] The election feature is shelved until the 2027 Washington local elections. Its canvas and guide stay as they are. **Reversed October 6 (founder): Ballot ships for the November 3 general election, on web, iOS and Android, behind `ballot_p0`; see the Ballot section.**
- [x] On a pickup evening the pickup leads the push; only a serious weather alert beats it. Nearby updates never lead an evening push.
- [x] Task reminders arrive the morning of the due day in the household's time zone; Support Train reminders use the train's local time.
- [x] The task-completed push doesn't name the task or the person on the lock screen. **Reversed November 5 (founder): notifications show their content and the phone hides it while locked; the task-completed push names the person and the task (see November 5 below).**

**Made by the founder on October 6.**

- [x] Launch checklist decisions D1, D2, D4, D5, D7 and D8 as recommended: the API at `api.pantopus.com` (staging `staging-api.pantopus.com`); new store listings named "Pantopus Home"; Stripe test mode during the pilot; a live Lob key and a real return address before production; no error monitoring in the pilot; one server carries staging and production. D6: staging already sends its email through Postmark (set up October 7), so production follows unless you say otherwise.
- [x] A homeowner whose ownership claim is pending can verify their address by postcard while the claim waits; they become a verified resident and ownership stays pending. Admin identity attestation was not chosen.

- [x] Holiday pickup moves: the founder confirmed the Vancouver, Washougal and Camas rules from the official sources (PR 1693 marks those rows official). Camas garbage on New Year's Day 2027 stays unconfirmed, and never pushes, until the founder's call to Camas Sanitation (section 2).

**Made by the founder on October 8.**

- [x] D3: use a fresh production Supabase project, preserve existing users' logins through a rehearsed Auth and matching-profile transfer, and leave April's other data in its old project. The founder created `pantopus-production` in Oregon under the Chrome production Supabase login, applied all 148 canonical migrations and created the private 25 MB `home-documents` bucket on October 9. It remains in the April project's Free organization; the founder chose to stay on Free for now and defer the 100 MB Storage buckets and Auth transfer. The founder later directed a public backend and web cutover before that transfer, accepting that April accounts cannot sign in to the new project yet. A separate organization is optional billing isolation, not a deployment prerequisite. The transfer procedure is in [the production Auth runbook](docs/release/production-auth-transfer.md).
- [x] October 9 production web release: defer Lob, Smarty and Google Address Validation credentials. The backend may start with the production-only provider deferral switch, but new address validation, Add Home without cached verification and postcard flows remain unavailable until these providers are set up. No mock mail is used on hosted deployments.
- [x] Place shows each person what their role and verification allow. A saved address shows public facts. A Home set up but not yet verified shows public facts and its own tools, with the Home's records and money signals locked behind "Verify your address by mail". A verified renter sees the Home's facts and systems, the rent band and neighbours' real rent, but no value or tax exemption. A verified owner sees the Home's facts, value, systems, the exemption check and the bill comparison, but no rent; while ownership is pending, value and the exemption say "Available once your ownership is confirmed." Household members see public facts and the Home's facts; guests and service providers see public facts only. Built on the web, iOS and Android (PRs 1847, 1848, 1849). Checked on the web with seven synthetic accounts, one per role and stage; on the Android emulator as a pending owner, a mail-verified pending owner, a renter, a guest and an owner; on the iOS simulator as a pending owner and a household member.

**Made on October 20 (AI assistant), from the [AI expansion proposal](https://claude.ai/artifact/DkyRkpcQ1bLL4ETb3DjqQ5).** You said "start phase 1 as proposed, using your recommendations for the open decisions and recording them"; these are those recommendations, and they settle the October 4 agent design's open decisions. Progress is in the AI assistant section below; the build contract is [docs/product/ai-assistant-phase1-contract-2026-10-20.md](docs/product/ai-assistant-phase1-contract-2026-10-20.md).

- [x] Build phase 1 now, not after the pilot's first read. The assistant reads what the person can see in Plans, Pulse, Place and Today, and fills in actions as cards that one tap sends through an endpoint the app already uses. It also knows how the app works and links to the right screen. No new tables and no new write paths. iOS and Android; the web keeps working unchanged.
- [x] Name and autonomy: "Pantopus" in the app and "Ask Pantopus" on buttons. Every action waits for the person's tap; standing rules wait for phase 3.
- [x] Where it appears: a box at the top of Today and an Ask button on Place, Nearby and Plans, inside each screen's current design. No tab changes. In chats with phase 2.
- [x] Languages: it answers in the language a person writes in; the app's own words stay English.
- [x] Reading a chat: only when the person asks inside that chat, only chats they're in, never in the background. Phase 2, after the privacy policy names AI processing and the Anthropic account's retention setting is confirmed.
- [x] Speaking first (phase 2): one morning brief and at most two nudges a day, held during quiet hours, each with "Fewer like this", and never about an unconfirmed pickup day.
- [x] New tables for phase 3: `AgentRequest`, `AgentStep` and `AgentMemoryItem` from the October 4 [system design](docs/product/pantopus-agent-system-design-2026-10-04.md), built when phase 2 ships. Memory is kept until the person deletes it, with a yearly reminder to review it. Standing rules (`AgentRule`) come later in phase 3.
- [x] Siri App Intents and the Android equivalent in phase 3, starting with five actions: next pickup, what's on today, start a plan, catch me up nearby, remind my household.
- [x] Model unchanged: Claude Sonnet 5.5 for everything (October 12). Claude Haiku 5.5 for background jobs stays your option.
- [x] Phase 2 starts as soon as phase 1 is merged, in fresh sessions that use the recommended defaults without waiting for you. Anything that reads chat messages waits for your privacy step. You said so on October 20.
- [ ] Yours before phase 1 ships: the privacy policy and the data inventory name AI processing and Anthropic as a processor, and you confirm the Anthropic account's data-retention setting. The draft wording is in [#2472](https://github.com/WangPantopus/skinny-pantopus/pull/2472): confirm the retention setting and Anthropic's terms there, then merge it. The backend half of phase 1 has been in production since October 20, so the policy is behind the code until #2472 merges.

- [x] Standing rules, with `AgentRule` as a fourth phase 3 table.

  - Accepting requests: each plan's own entry rules already do it.
  - Answering guests in the plan chat: it reads chats, so it waits for your privacy step.

This replaces the earlier six-tab answer for phone web.

The note, signature, photo and voice greeting are seen only in Pantopus.

**Made by the founder on November 2 (links through an app install).** You asked whether an invite is lost when someone installs the app before opening it. It was: a link tapped before Pantopus is installed opens its web page, and the App Store or Google Play then opened the new app with nothing from it. You said "Yes, build it".

- [x] A link tapped before the install now survives it, for friends' invites, plan invitation links, Support Trains and posts.
  - The web page's store buttons carry the link ([PR 3149](https://github.com/WangPantopus/skinny-pantopus/pull/3149)).
  - Android, no tap: Google Play hands the link to the app on its first launch, through its install referrer ([PR 3173](https://github.com/WangPantopus/skinny-pantopus/pull/3173)).
  - iPhone, one tap: Apple passes nothing through the App Store. The page copies the link, and the app's first screen offers it back with Apple's Paste button, which shows no paste alert ([PR 3162](https://github.com/WangPantopus/skinny-pantopus/pull/3162)).
  - The link opens as if it had been tapped: a friend's invite opens Create account with "<name> invited you" and credits them, and a plan opens once they sign up or sign in.
  - Safari's install banner is off the friend-invite page and off plan invitation links, because installing from it drops the link.
  - No server change, no new tables.
- [ ] Yours, with the next store builds: on a phone without Pantopus, open a friend's invite link, get the app from the page's button and open it. Android should open Create account with "<name> invited you". The iPhone should offer Paste, then do the same. Try a plan's "Text an invite" link the same way: the plan should open once you sign up or sign in. A store install can't be done on a simulator or an emulator, so this is the one step not checked yet.

**Made by the founder on November 5 (notification previews).** You sent two iOS banners reading "New message — Open Pantopus to read it." and asked that notifications show their content "as long as they unlocked the screen ... just like how they see everything from other apps".

- [x] Notifications show their content. A locked phone hides it by its own setting, as for other apps: iOS Show Previews (When Unlocked by default; chats then read "New message") and Android's sensitive-content setting (chats then read "New message", the rest "New notification"). No new in-app setting.
  - Chat: the sender and the message, or a group's name with "Sender: message". A business-inbox notice says what its bell row says ("New message for <business>", "<sender>: <message>").
  - Task assigned and task completed name the person and the task ("Sam R. completed a task", "Test for radon"); their bell rows stay generic, because a bell row outlives the reader's access to the Home. The Mail Day copy names who the mail is from (that job doesn't run today).
  - Unchanged: people are named as the chat or the Home names them (never a staff member's or a private name), and blocks, mutes, read and deletion checks and deliberately anonymous notices stay.
  - PRs: backend [3431](https://github.com/WangPantopus/skinny-pantopus/pull/3431), iOS [3429](https://github.com/WangPantopus/skinny-pantopus/pull/3429), Android [3430](https://github.com/WangPantopus/skinny-pantopus/pull/3430). The backend change has been in production since 07:04 UTC on November 6 (11:04 PM Pacific on November 5; Deploy Backend [run 42178189692](https://github.com/WangPantopus/skinny-pantopus/actions/runs/42178189692), commit `92a0d3596`, `/health` healthy) and reaches today's store apps at once; the iOS placeholder and the Android lock-screen stand-in come with the next store builds. Real Apple and Google delivery and physical phones aren't checked yet.
- [x] A push for an account that isn't signed in on the phone shows none of its content. It arrives after signing out when it was already queued at Apple, or while someone else is signed in. The iOS extension `PantopusNotificationService` shows "New message" or "New notification" instead, without the link: iOS [3494](https://github.com/WangPantopus/skinny-pantopus/pull/3494), backend [3498](https://github.com/WangPantopus/skinny-pantopus/pull/3498). If the phone can't say who is signed in (the app was updated and not opened yet), a push stays as it came. It comes with the next store build. iOS doesn't run extensions for simulated pushes, so a real push going through it isn't checked yet.
- [ ] Yours, with TestFlight 2.0.12 (91): sign in on your iPhone, turn on airplane mode, have someone message you, sign out, then turn airplane mode off. The banner should read "New message". While you're signed in, messages should still show their real content.
- [ ] Optional, yours: hiding such a push altogether, rather than showing "New message", needs Apple to grant the notification filtering entitlement, by a request form on Apple's site. Without it an extension can only change a push's text.

A session the server ends removes that phone's tokens ([3436](https://github.com/WangPantopus/skinny-pantopus/pull/3436)). Android shows Plans pushes when the app is closed ([3444](https://github.com/WangPantopus/skinny-pantopus/pull/3444)). Every push names its recipient, so Android shows neighbor-message pushes; ten more types follow the Gig and Home switches; a declined join request names nobody ([3459](https://github.com/WangPantopus/skinny-pantopus/pull/3459)). "Mail not picked up" opens Sent ([3502](https://github.com/WangPantopus/skinny-pantopus/pull/3502)).

A simulator can't show this. If Apple keeps accepting an old token, the same account could get duplicates, and another account's pushes could still arrive; their content is stripped and tapping one is refused.

Assistant: held nudges aren't dropped once read or no longer readable, "two a day" counts rows, not pushes, a rule's pickup push is dropped from 9 PM, and a double pickup push is possible when the stored briefing zone is empty.

**The November 4 audit PRs, reviewed November 6–7.** You asked for the 15 PRs left open by the November 4 audits to be checked in the real apps, fixed where needed and merged, or dropped. Four sessions rebased each onto master, reproduced its problem on master, checked the fix against a local backend and database, and merged it on green CI.

- [x] Server, released: saving weekly hours is one transaction, so a bad time no longer wipes the week ([3323](https://github.com/WangPantopus/skinny-pantopus/pull/3323)). The hours routes check that the location belongs to the business ([3326](https://github.com/WangPantopus/skinny-pantopus/pull/3326)); before, one business's owner could read another's hours and add or delete its special hours. Deleting a catalog category ungroups its items and repairs old rows ([3320](https://github.com/WangPantopus/skinny-pantopus/pull/3320)). Public reviews return owner replies ([3316](https://github.com/WangPantopus/skinny-pantopus/pull/3316)).
- [x] Web, live: the weekly-hours editor loads on its own and keeps split intervals and notes ([3324](https://github.com/WangPantopus/skinny-pantopus/pull/3324)) and fits a phone ([3325](https://github.com/WangPantopus/skinny-pantopus/pull/3325)). Public business reviews show owner replies and recover from a failed load ([3316](https://github.com/WangPantopus/skinny-pantopus/pull/3316)). The phase 3 privacy policy ([3147](https://github.com/WangPantopus/skinny-pantopus/pull/3147)).
- [x] iOS: Manage services opens the catalog ([3319](https://github.com/WangPantopus/skinny-pantopus/pull/3319)); the business editor checks current permissions before each save and keeps drafts ([3322](https://github.com/WangPantopus/skinny-pantopus/pull/3322)); business hours can be edited, and a day with two intervals no longer crashes the editor ([3327](https://github.com/WangPantopus/skinny-pantopus/pull/3327)). Open 24 hours now saves 00:00–23:59.

- [x] Follow-ups from the review, merged: Android hours keep the rest of the week, notes and second intervals ([3624](https://github.com/WangPantopus/skinny-pantopus/pull/3624)). A saved `24:00` close reads as midnight on the web ([3613](https://github.com/WangPantopus/skinny-pantopus/pull/3613)) and in the iOS picker ([3626](https://github.com/WangPantopus/skinny-pantopus/pull/3626)). The business profile's open state reads every interval on a day ([3614](https://github.com/WangPantopus/skinny-pantopus/pull/3614)). Android's public business page shows owner replies and loads reviews on their own ([3627](https://github.com/WangPantopus/skinny-pantopus/pull/3627)). A clean iOS build finishes on Xcode 26.5 ([3611](https://github.com/WangPantopus/skinny-pantopus/pull/3611)).
- [ ] Follow-ups still open on November 7: Android owner screens show only what the person may do ([3630](https://github.com/WangPantopus/skinny-pantopus/pull/3630)); Android's hours row reads `24:00` as midnight ([3635](https://github.com/WangPantopus/skinny-pantopus/pull/3635)); Discover's open state reads every interval ([3629](https://github.com/WangPantopus/skinny-pantopus/pull/3629)), a migration that reaches production when it merges.
- [ ] Yours: the iOS and Android changes reach people with the next store builds. The 2.0.12 builds were cut from `76be4468d`, before all of them, including the iOS editor crash fix.
- [x] Yours: Vercel's build rate limit fails most master deploys, so pantopus.com catches up only when one gets through. Vercel's link on those failures offers its Pro plan. (November 23: Vercel built each of the last six master commits.)
- [ ] Smaller problems found and not fixed, each a small change if you want it:
  - Android: the HTTP client re-sends a POST when a pooled connection drops after sending it, so a create without a client request id can be made twice (access codes are handled since [3328](https://github.com/WangPantopus/skinny-pantopus/pull/3328)); after a lost delete reply the access-code form says "Couldn't find that access code" though the delete worked; Support Train's Add date defaults to the UTC date, its window pickers show a date step they ignore, its choice chips are clipped at 200% text, and a helper is offered all three ways to help whatever the slot's mode.
  - iOS: Preview after a direct editor link returns to Place; the editor still shows a drawn map, a VERIFY ADDRESS badge and a Hide exact address toggle; catalog rows swap places after an edit when they share a sort order; a service with no price unit shows "$15/"; "Your daily briefings" clips Got it on an iPhone SE.
  - Hours: the API takes at most 7 rows a week, so adding a day to a week that already has 7 rows is refused with the raw message "hours must contain less than or equal to 7 items". Nothing is lost.
- [ ] Not checked: physical phones, and these flows on production. There, only the privacy page and the API's health were read.

The table cloth ships as WebP (178 KB) instead of JPEG.

**Made by the founder on November 7 (Plans without a verified address).** You sent the iPhone composer's playdate preview, which refused with "Verify your address to host a playdate.", and said to make it available for users who are on Pantopus. Progress is in the Nearby Plans section below.

- [x] Plans asks for no verified address. Anyone on Pantopus sees playdates, asks to join one and hosts one, like the other kinds; invitations and playdate links open for them too. What stays: a playdate meets in a public place within 3 miles of where the host lives or is now, families within about 3 miles ask with their kids' ages, the host approves each family, parents stay and only ages are shared. A verified address still shows the "Verified" badge and still lets verified neighbors in right away where the host chose that (never for playdates). This replaces the verified-address part of the October 12, October 20 and October 31 decisions.
- [ ] The apps on phones today (2.0.12 and earlier) still send someone without a verified address from the playdate card to "Verify your address" until the next store builds. Seeing playdates, asking to join, invitations and playdate links work for them as soon as the backend is deployed.

**Made by the founder on November 11 (help writing a Christmas card).** You asked whether Pantopus AI should design Christmas cards. The recommendation was no AI pictures this season and an AI helper for the note instead; you said "Yes, plan it", then "Yes, build it".

- [x] "Help me write it" on the card's write step: one optional line to mention, three notes (heartfelt, light, brief) within the 300 letters, pick one and edit it, Undo. It never invents memories or facts about the people, follows the picture's mood, and writes in the sender's language. The card carries no "written by AI" mark, like AI-assisted posts.
- [x] A new launch key, `card_note_help`, on the server only, which needs `mailbox` and `holiday_cards` on too. It starts off. No new table and no migration.
- [x] A list still sends one note to everyone, so for three or more people the helper writes a note that names no one. A different note per person is not built.
- [ ] Not this season: AI-made card pictures. "Draw our photo in this card's style" would need a picture vendor and your decision on sending family photos to it.

**Made by the founder on November 15 (Plans for the holidays, and paying businesses).** You asked whether Plans is strong enough for Thanksgiving, Christmas, New Year's, birthdays and team outings, and where businesses fit. Design only for now; the documents are [docs/product/plans-holidays](docs/product/plans-holidays/README.md). Progress is in "Plans for the holidays" below.

- [x] The additions, agreed: answer from a link without the app, bigger groups with adults and kids, the bring list, occasions (Thanksgiving dinner, Friendsgiving, Holiday party, Gift exchange, New Year's Eve, Birthday), then find a date, chip in, gift exchange, photos afterwards and groups.
- [x] Businesses post the plans they host and give deals, including a group deal that unlocks for everyone when enough people join by a time.
- [x] The Plans screen stays clean: one thing on the right of a row, no banners, extras behind switches inside a plan, one deal a plan from fixed kinds. Retention matters most.
- [x] Guests pay a business through Pantopus, with tips. The sixteen payment decisions P1 to P16, all as recommended ([the list](docs/product/plans-holidays/02-payments.md#12-the-decisions-accepted-november-15)): a price to join, then the bill at the place; 15% card fee included on a spot for a guest Pantopus brought and 5% plus the card fee for the business's own; 5% and 1% plus the card fee on a bill; no cut of a tip; a group price charges first and returns the difference; paid plans in person only; payout 48 hours after the plan ends.
- [x] This round is design: no code in the designing session. When the design is confirmed, the build is done by separate sessions on Opus 5.5 at Extra High.
- [x] The calls the documents make (H1 to H11 in the design, K1 to K10 in the contract): all as recommended. "Yes, let's start" for the people track, built by new sessions on Opus 5.5 at Extra High, each given the instructions, the design and the screens for exactly what it builds.

**Made by the founder on November 15 (one card to many people by email).** You asked whether a Christmas card can go to several people who aren't on Pantopus by email, to make sure it does and works, and what happens when it fails.

- [x] A list (digital stamps on) can go to as many new email addresses as there are stamps for. Before, only 3 new addresses a week went and the rest showed "Won't be sent". Cards taken back still count as new addresses for that week, so the 3 free stamps can't be sent, taken back and sent again to email stranger after stranger. With stamps off the old rule stays (3 new addresses a week). The same address still gets one card in 30 days, unless the earlier card was taken back before its email went out.
- [x] Each person gets their own email with only their own address in it; nobody on a list sees who else got one (unchanged).
- [x] An email that doesn't go out is no longer silent. The card keeps whether its email went; the first send gets a second try a moment later, then the server tries every 10 minutes, 5 tries in all within about an hour, then tells the sender once (one notice for several cards). Sent says "The email didn't go out" with the address as typed, and offers Email it again or Take it back (the stamp comes back). No new table, no migration, no new key.
- [x] Later the same day you asked for the same three arrivals by email as for people, and to keep Right away too ("yes build it and keep the right away too"). A card by email now has four choices: with the morning mail (the email goes at the sender's next 7 a.m.), on Christmas morning (the email goes at 7 a.m. on December 25), sealed until Christmas (the email goes now, as before) and right away. The 7 a.m. is the sender's, because the other person's time zone isn't known until they join. Nothing is emailed before that time: taking the card back first means no email at all, and the stamp comes back. The card's own row is the queue (no new table, no migration, no new key); the worker sends what is due every 5 minutes, so emails land by about 7:05. Apps from before this keep their two email choices.
- [ ] Not covered: an address that the mail server accepts but later bounces (a typo at a real domain). Pantopus doesn't receive Postmark's bounce reports, so such a card looks emailed. Catching those needs a Postmark bounce webhook, which needs your Postmark settings.

**Asked by the founder on November 16 (more sign-ups from cards).** Five changes, built by three sessions. This entry covers the sender's side; the card's emails (its picture in the email, one reminder) and the card's link page (its picture before sign-up, sign-up with a one-time code) have their own PRs.

- [x] pantopus.com/cards now says what sending costs and how the card gets there, under its first line: "Your first 3 cards are free, plus one more each time someone joins from your card." and "It arrives in their Pantopus mailbox. If they aren't on Pantopus yet, they get an email from you." The "3" comes from the server (`free_cards` in `GET /api/mailbox/cards/designs`), only while digital stamps are on for everyone (`MAIL_STAMPS_ENABLED=true`). With stamps off the first line is "Sending a card is free."
- [x] A sender gets one free card when a card they sent by email or by link brings someone in: the person made their account after the card was sealed, and has now opened it. Digital stamps on only, while cards can still be sent. One for each new person, ever, whoever's card they open first. The sender's "Your Christmas card arrived" notice adds "You have one more free card to send.", and the Stamps wallet on iPhone and Android lists it as "Your card brought someone in · +1 free" (older app builds show "Stamps · 1"). Every card costs a stamp, so this gives back at most what those cards cost. No new table, no migration, no new key: it is one more row in the stamp ledger (`join:<new person>`).
- [ ] Not built: telling senders about the free card inside the apps before they send (the "Add their email" step would be the place). It needs the same sentence on the web, iPhone and Android.

**Made by the founder on November 16 (creator links for Christmas cards).** You are emailing Christian creators about the Christmas cards and want to pay each one a share of the stamp books bought by people who join through their link. You approved the plan with "Yes, build it": server and web only, no phone app changes, no new table.

- [x] An admin page, pantopus.com/app/admin/creators (admins only; anyone else sees "Admins only"). Find the creator's Pantopus account by email address or username and type a code of 6 to 12 letters and digits, such as GRACEFM. Each account has one code. The page refuses a code with spaces or marks, a code that is already anyone's invite code (in any letter case), and a second code for the same account, and says why. Codes are kept in capitals, and a link typed in any case still counts (gracefm, GraceFM). Everyone's own invite links are unchanged.
- [x] The link to give a creator is `https://pantopus.com/cards?ref=GRACEFM` (or one card: `https://pantopus.com/cards/<card>?ref=GRACEFM`). Signing up from it on the web, by email or with Google or Apple, records the new account against the creator, as a friend's invite link does. People who already had an account, or who joined through the creator's own personal invite link, don't count.
- [x] On a phone, the card pages' store button now carries a creator's code through the install, for creator codes only: Google Play hands it to the Android app, and the iPhone copies it for the app's Paste button. The app opens Create account with "Grace Radio invited you to Pantopus" and the code filled in. The apps can carry only one link, so that person starts on Place instead of on the cards; the words under the button say to tap "Send a Christmas card" in the menu after creating the account. Every other card link, including the ordinary shared card links that carry the sharer's own invite code, a code nobody has, and no code, keeps the button exactly as it was (it carries that card's sending screen).
- [x] The same page lists, for each creator: the code and its link (with a copy button), people who joined through it, how many of them bought books, books bought by size (5, 20, 50), refunded books (shown, not counted), sales at list price, and the amount owed at the percentage you type in the box (not saved). Counts and sums only: no buyer's name, email or account. Sales are list prices ($2.99, $7.99, $14.99) before Apple, Google and Stripe fees.
- [x] Checked against a local server and database with made-up accounts: gave a code on the page (finding the account sends the typed email address in the request body, never in the address); signed up from `/cards?ref=gracefm` on the web; a Google or Apple first sign-in carrying the code (the server call the apps make after the provider; the provider screens themselves weren't run); the iPhone simulator (Paste, Create account, credited) and the Android emulator (the install referrer as Google Play sends it, Create account, credited); then recorded stamp books, one of them refunded, plus books bought by people who must not count. The page showed 2 joined, 3 books, $18.97 and $3.79 at 20%, the same after a reload, and "Admins only" to a non-admin. The store button was compared with the old one on iPhone and Android for every kind of link: creator codes in any letter case change it; an ordinary person's code (as every shared card carries), a code nobody has and no code leave it exactly as before, and a web sign-up from an ordinary shared card still credits the person who shared it.
- [ ] Not checked: real App Store and Google Play installs, real purchases, and anything on staging or production.
- [ ] Yours: once this is on production, give each creator a code on pantopus.com/app/admin/creators (your account needs the admin role) and send them their link. When it's time to pay, type your percentage on the page and pay them yourself; the page keeps no record of payments. Stamp books must be on for the public for there to be sales.

**Asked by the founder on November 16 (a bigger card, photos that open, and Live Photos on cards).** You sent two screenshots of a card on your iPhone: the card was small and hard to read while much of the screen was empty. You asked for the card to use the screen with nothing hidden, for an attached photo to open at full size, and, if possible, for a Live Photo to play as it does in Pulse and chat. All of it is on staging (`dev`); none of it is in production until you promote staging.

- [x] The card is as wide as the phone on iPhone, Android and web. A card with a photo or voice greeting used to be laid out small (344 of 440 points on an iPhone 17 Pro Max; it is 422 now). With its picture up it lies under the whole flap; turned over, it moves up and its photo and greeting come under it, never over the card's printing ([4271](https://github.com/WangPantopus/skinny-pantopus/pull/4271), [4281](https://github.com/WangPantopus/skinny-pantopus/pull/4281), [4278](https://github.com/WangPantopus/skinny-pantopus/pull/4278)).
- [x] A tap on the attached photo opens it over the whole screen: pinch or double-tap to enlarge, drag to move around it, pull down or ✕ to close. On the card, on Look it over and in Sent. A covered photo (from someone who isn't a connection) still needs its own tap first.
- [x] After the first turn the card says "double-tap the card to enlarge it". The card could already be enlarged up to three times, but nothing said so.
- [x] A Live Photo attached on the iPhone keeps its few seconds of video, with its sound ([server 4280](https://github.com/WangPantopus/skinny-pantopus/pull/4280), [iPhone 4285](https://github.com/WangPantopus/skinny-pantopus/pull/4285), [web 4287](https://github.com/WangPantopus/skinny-pantopus/pull/4287), [Android 4290](https://github.com/WangPantopus/skinny-pantopus/pull/4290)). The person who gets it sees a small LIVE mark; the opened photo moves once, and holding it or its LIVE button plays it again. The sender can choose "Send as a still" instead. The clip's location and camera tags are removed on the phone and again on the server, and only the card's two people can read it. No new table and no new switch: it is on wherever `holiday_card_memories` is. The contract's [section 13](docs/product/christmas-card-memories-contract-2026-11-02.md) has the rules.
- [x] A card's photo is kept at up to 2400 pixels on its long side (it was 1600), so an opened photo stays sharp when it's enlarged. You chose this on November 16. A stored photo is a little under twice the size it was (260 to 640 KB on five sample photographs). Cards already sent keep the photo they have. An Android phone with an older build still sends 1600 until it updates; the iPhone and the web already send enough.
- [ ] Not done: attaching a Live Photo from Android or the web (their pickers give a still; cards sent from an iPhone play on all three).
- [ ] Not checked: physical phones, a Live Photo taken by a real iPhone camera (the simulator's was made by hand), sound by ear, Safari, and the open iPhone Duo. Everything was checked in the iPhone simulator, an Android emulator and the browser against a local server and database.

**Made by the founder on November 14 (sharing a Christmas card as a video).** You asked for a second way to share a card, a video of the whole experience, and worried about people sharing a private note. You approved one "Share this card" button with Picture | Video in its sheet, videos that never show a note or a name, and the build ("Yes, plan the build", then "yes, finish them please" after the first three videos).

**Made by the founder on November 13 (the tab order, ways into a Christmas card, and the first screen without an address).** You asked whether to reorder the tabs to win attention and get Christmas cards sent, approved the eight [drawings](https://claude.ai/artifact/JmQRCAsKi2uXTFoHVsk2jG) (private) with "all and including board 8", and said "Let's start". You asked for the sun's position and the time until sunrise or sunset on both screens.

**Made by the founder on November 14 (local news leaves Pulse; Today gets two halves).** You showed the curator account filling the staging Pulse feed with rewritten news posts and asked whether to keep it. Detail and what is left are in the section "Around you" at the end of this file.

- [x] The curator account's posts leave Pulse: the list and the map show people's posts only. The Sports lane keeps its own.
- [x] Today has two halves under a big switch, drawn as Pulse's List | Map: **At home** (Today as it was) and **Around you** (local headlines). You asked for the split top tab on Place or Today; it is on Today, because Place is the private page about a home and Today already works from a city alone.
- [x] Around you shows the outlet's own headline, the outlet's name and a link to the story. Nothing is rewritten and no Claude call is made. On November 15 you asked for the pictures these posts had in Pulse: each post now carries the story's own pictures (up to four), shown from the outlet's address, never copied. A picture the outlet has since taken down is left out (the card lays out the ones that load, and shows no picture area when none do) instead of leaving a gray box, on the web, iPhone and Android ([#4134](https://github.com/WangPantopus/skinny-pantopus/pull/4134)).
- [x] The switch shows only where there is something to list; elsewhere Today is unchanged.
- [x] One backend switch, `AROUND_YOU` (on for staging, off in production until you set it). No new table or migration.
- [x] Asked the same evening, built: every Around you item is a real post by the Pantopus curator account, drawn with the Pulse card. People react with emojis, reply in threads (a reply to a reply to a reply steps in under the one it answers), save, repost and share it, and "Read at <outlet>" opens the story. The posts themselves list only on Today; you said a repost may carry one into Pulse, and it does, for the reposter's connections.
- [x] Asked November 15: every Around you layout on the iPhone Duo, "whether it's folded, unfolded level, unfolded upright" ([#4134](https://github.com/WangPantopus/skinny-pantopus/pull/4134), iPhone only). Folded, it is the phone's page. Open and held level, the list is the page left of the hinge, under Today's title and switch, and the post you open is the page right of it at the screen's full height; nothing is drawn across the hinge. Open and upright, the list and the post's page are one centered column. Folding, unfolding or turning mid-task keeps the open post, a reply being typed and its keyboard, and an open picture viewer or story sheet (the viewer goes back to the picture it was opened on). Checked on Apple's Duo simulator, not on a real Duo. Detail in "Around you".
- [x] Asked with it: repost with a quote (your own words over the headline). The Pulse post-buttons work built "Repost with your words" for every Pulse post: its server half ([#4108](https://github.com/WangPantopus/skinny-pantopus/pull/4108)), its web half, its iPhone half ([#4128](https://github.com/WangPantopus/skinny-pantopus/pull/4128)) and its Android half ([#4136](https://github.com/WangPantopus/skinny-pantopus/pull/4136)) are on `dev`. An Around you card opens the same chooser on the web, on iPhone (since [#4134](https://github.com/WangPantopus/skinny-pantopus/pull/4134)) and on Android (since [#4140](https://github.com/WangPantopus/skinny-pantopus/pull/4140)): ↻ offers "Repost" (or "Undo repost") or "Repost with your words", and the second opens the Pulse composer with the headline attached.

**Made by the founder on November 14 (Pulse reposts and the post menu).** You tapped Repost on a Pulse post on your phone and saw only the number beside ↻ change ("That's nothing"), and asked that every button in the post menu do what it says. You approved the two-step plan ("Please build") and set the bar: a repost shows up in the feed with the person who reposted it and in that person's profile Posts, Repost offers "repost with a quote", and Pulse should beat X's and Facebook's feeds. Detail and what is left are in the section "Pulse reposts and repost with your words" at the end of this file.

- [x] A repost shows up: the post moves to the top of Nearby and Connections with "↻ You reposted" (or the names of your connections who reposted it), and is listed in the reposter's profile Posts and in My posts' new Reposts tab.
- [x] Repost opens two choices: Repost (or Undo repost) and Repost with your words, which opens the composer with the original post as a card under your words.
- [x] Save says "Saved." with View (My posts, Saved tab); Hide has Undo; "Not helpful here" removes the post for you, with Undo.

**Made by the founder on November 14 (Plans: private, repeating, online, more kinds).** You showed the empty Plans tab and asked how to make it the place people come to when they plan something with others: repeating plans, more categories, online plans, a private plan only the invited people can see, and no "neighbor" in the words ("whatever we use let's not use neighbor"). Detail, the checks and what is left are in the section "Plans: private, repeating, online and four new kinds" at the end of this file.

- [x] Repeating plans: every week on chosen days, every 2 weeks, or every month, until a date or for a number of times, a year at most. Each date is its own plan, posted when the one before ends; the people who came are asked again each time.

**Made by the founder on November 17 (faster home verification).** You asked whether verifying a home is too complex next to Nextdoor and others, and whether a couple of taps could do it safely. The research found that today's path is a four-step form and a mailed code, while Nextdoor verifies most people in under a minute by phone or location. You said "Yes, draw it up", then "Yes, all nine" to the decisions on [the design page](https://claude.ai/artifact/HWZu7m6B7p8fwo9KXSsBmB), then started the build: "yes, use Opus 5.5, with Extra High reasoning … And at the end, use Fable 5.2 to verify and check on everything. Please make sure we have the auto address complete available everywhere when it is related to addresses, as users typing." Progress is in the section "Faster home verification" below.

  - verified by a fast check: the Verified mark, messaging homes near you, the home's details and what others near you pay in rent;
  - verified by mail or document, as today: all of that plus residency letters, home Mail and owner powers.

  8. A fast check stands for 12 months and is renewed with one tap at home.

There's no undo, and old copies can stay in the CDN and on viewers' devices. Details are in the [photo-location handoff](docs/photo-location-place-picker-handoff-2026-11-05.md).

If the Console asks you to declare the permission, say it reads the location in files the person picks, to post to that place; the app doesn't request `READ_MEDIA_IMAGES` or `READ_MEDIA_VIDEO`. Google's [photo and video policy](https://support.google.com/googleplay/android-developer/answer/14115180) names only those two, and we found nothing saying whether this one needs a declaration. Then check a real phone with that build, ideally Android 14–16 with a current Google Play system update and Android 17 if you can: take a photo with the camera app with location on, attach it in the post composer, and tap "Add location". A "Photo location" chip with no question means that phone shows the location. "Post where this photo was taken?" means it hides it: tap Continue, allow, and the chip should appear without attaching again. Declining should change nothing and not ask again. Details are in the [photo-location handoff](docs/photo-location-place-picker-handoff-2026-11-05.md).

## 4. The pilot

- [ ] Start with five households once one full journey works on the hosted backend and a scheduled reminder has reached a physical phone. Expand to 30 to 50 after two pickup weeks with no wrong or missed reminder. Eight weeks per household.
- [ ] Measure activation within seven days; reminders sent, acted on, completed and found helpful; discoveries; household follow-through; week-four and week-eight return counted as handling a responsibility, not opening the app; founder minutes per household; zero unconfirmed dates pushed as confirmed.
- [ ] Interview each household in weeks two and eight: "What did Pantopus help you notice or handle that would otherwise have slipped through?"
- [ ] Decide from the numbers what comes next from section 5.

## 4A. Crew Day by hand, beside the pilot

Phases 0 and 1 of the [Street Organizer launch plan](docs/product/street-organizer-design-2026-09-27.md#launch-plan). Founder-run, with no app build. Each gate must hold before the next step.

**Since October 22 this is the first money feature (section 0).** Households pay the crew's business invoice; Pantopus keeps its fee from the crew's payout. The crew call, crew terms, organizer guide, sign-up sheet, messages, pricing worksheet and day-of checklist are in the [Crew Day Kit](https://claude.ai/artifact/BfXLe9ThWTrFk92kR76Knz) (private).

- [x] Close the business fee leak and check the invoice flow (October 22). [#2571](https://github.com/WangPantopus/skinny-pantopus/pull/2571) and [#2572](https://github.com/WangPantopus/skinny-pantopus/pull/2572) merged as `2fc6ec5ed` and are in production: a fee under 10% applies only after Pantopus verifies the business type, and the crew is told when an invoice is refunded. In Stripe test mode a household paid a crew's $160 invoice on the web, iOS and Android, and the crew was paid $136 (15%); a founding business was paid $90 of $100 (10%). Not checked: Stripe's own onboarding form, live keys, and the crew's screens on Android.
- [ ] What a street day needs from households and crews, found by that check and written into the kit: a crew gets 10% only after you approve its document and it claims a founding slot in the iOS or Android app (15% otherwise); a crew can invoice only people who have messaged its business page; households pay from the invoice notification, because no screen lists received invoices; refunds are an admin step (no refund button). The session proposed two fixes as cards: a list of received invoices on all three apps, and a server check that refuses invoices to people a business doesn't know.
- [x] October 26: live payment configuration and the payment schedules are enabled ([checklist P11](docs/release/prod-config-checklist.md#p11-live-payments-for-crew-day-founder)). The final 2.0.7 candidates use the live publishable key: iOS build 87 is waiting for review and Android version code 29 is submitted for production review. They replace the 2.0.6 candidates; public release remains manual on both stores. A real card payment, refund, delayed transfer and bank payout remain founder acceptance checks.
- [ ] The first street day on business invoices. Log homes, price, what Pantopus kept and your hours in the kit's street day log.

- [ ] Phase 0, now to mid-October: talk to five to eight local crews about route savings, per-home prices, weather rules and paying for postcards. Pick one or two exterior services.
- [ ] Gate 1: crews confirm the route savings and accept the rules for payment, cancellation, weather and problems.
- [ ] Phase 1, this fall: Crew Days on two or three streets in Camas or Washougal, growing toward the design's five to ten. Use crew-funded postcards with per-home codes and the tools chosen in section 0. Track founder minutes and cost per Crew Day.
- [ ] Gate 2: invitations convert into bookings, crews want more routes, problems are resolved within the promise, and each Crew Day earns money after all costs.
- [ ] Offer the app to Crew Day households after their job is done. They can join the pilot in section 4.
- [ ] On a street that completes a Crew Day, test one porch coffee with a resident host.

First decide who receives the money (the recipient, the organizer, or Pantopus holding it), whether it waits until the Train ends, any platform fee, what happens when the recipient has no account, and whether organizers see anonymous amounts ([audit](docs/support-train-payments-audit.md)). Recommended order: first let the organizer add an outside fundraiser link (no money passes through Pantopus); in-app gifts through Stripe only after those decisions, with the same test-card checks as invoices.

- [ ] The Pantopus Agent, in four stages, each only after the one before passes its gate ([product design](docs/product/pantopus-agent-product-design-2026-10-04.md), [system design](docs/product/pantopus-agent-system-design-2026-10-04.md)). On October 20 the AI assistant section below took over Stage 1 and parts of Stages 3 and 4, in three phases; the people layer (Stage 2) and selling stay as designed here:
  - [ ] Stage 1, Know: one box on the Today tab answering from what Pantopus already knows, with sources, and tasks and reminders after one tap. In the pilot area first.
  - [ ] Stage 2, Ask people: questions routed to people who opted in to help, built once together with the Street Organizer's small asks.
  - [ ] Stage 3, Act for you: quotes, bookings and selling with approval, once marketplace and open gigs return. This is also the paid household assistant ("take this off my plate").
  - [ ] Stage 4, Anywhere: groups and topics with "Catch me up", visiting mode, translation, then other countries.

The 2027 local-election work it was shelved for stays parked ([build guide](docs/ballot-build-guide-2026-09-23.md), [review](docs/ballot-product-review-2026-09-23.md) and the design canvas kept).
- Creator network and Places: ten conversations first (five hobbyist photographers or hikers, five creators), then decide in late November.

For Ballot this supersedes the October 3 lines that shelve the election feature until 2027 ([section 6](#6-dropped-parked-or-shelved), the [build guide](docs/ballot-build-guide-2026-09-23.md)) and make new builds mobile only (the [pilot brief](docs/mobile-pilot-build-brief-2026-10-03.md)). The pre-launch review of PR #429 found defects on all three platforms and in the data; they are repaired on the branch ([plan §10.6](docs/ballot-implementation-plan-2026-09-24.md#106-pre-launch-repairs--october-6-2026)). On October 8 the branch was rebased onto master, checked on the web, an iOS simulator and an Android emulator, and repaired where master's newer code met it (guests and service providers get no Ballot card; the stale limit holds during a provider cooldown): [plan §10.7](docs/ballot-implementation-plan-2026-09-24.md#107-rebase-onto-master-and-verification--october-8-2026).

The server now refuses all three for a plan's post ([#3144](https://github.com/WangPantopus/skinny-pantopus/pull/3144)), and the web ([#3145](https://github.com/WangPantopus/skinny-pantopus/pull/3145)), iPhone ([#3146](https://github.com/WangPantopus/skinny-pantopus/pull/3146)) and Android ([#3174](https://github.com/WangPantopus/skinny-pantopus/pull/3174)) menus show **Open plan** instead, which opens the plan, where Change and Cancel tell everyone. Checked on the web, a simulator and an emulator against a local stack: Open plan from My posts, the post page and the profile card; a time moved from the plan asked the guest "Still coming?"; a plan cancelled there told its guest. The phones get Open plan with their next store builds; until then the old menu meets the server's refusal and the plan stays.

## Pulse (added October 13)

Decided October 13 (section 0). The [build kit](docs/product/pulse-build/README.md) has the spec, the API and data contract, the delivery plan, the verification kit and one brief per stream. Six sessions work in parallel: PB backend and data, PI iOS, PA Android, PS safety and composer checks, PC channels and public web, PV verification and release. Each keeps its status in `docs/product/pulse-build/status/`; PV keeps the [board](docs/product/pulse-build/status/BOARD.md). There is no flag: slices merge in the order the kit sets, and you choose when to deploy (backend, then web, then app builds) and cut builds.

**Paused October 15 (section 0):** the redesign is behind the launch-cut key `pulse_redesign`, off for launch, and the apps ship the October 13 Pulse. The three running streams (PB, PI, PA) stop after their switch PRs and leave their open PRs as drafts. Everything below resumes after launch.

## AI assistant (added October 20)

The contract the backend, iOS and Android sessions build against is [docs/product/ai-assistant-phase1-contract-2026-10-20.md](docs/product/ai-assistant-phase1-contract-2026-10-20.md). The server sends the new cards only to app builds that ask for them, so released apps and the web keep working while the apps catch up.

Merged October 20 ([#2467](https://github.com/WangPantopus/skinny-pantopus/pull/2467)) and released to production. Its Place and Today reads gave the model no sections until [#2481](https://github.com/WangPantopus/skinny-pantopus/pull/2481) fixed them the same day. Today's apps and the web get the better answers; the cards and link buttons wait for the new app builds.

It reaches phones with the next store build.

The apps now send the card list they already send to the chat on the history request too (`?cards=pickup_schedule,action,link`): iOS in [#2482](https://github.com/WangPantopus/skinny-pantopus/pull/2482), Android in [#2483](https://github.com/WangPantopus/skinny-pantopus/pull/2483). The route then leaves out action cards unless `action` is listed and link buttons unless `link` is listed ([#2483](https://github.com/WangPantopus/skinny-pantopus/pull/2483); contract sections 3, 4.1 and 5). So the released 2.0.1 apps no longer get a card another build made back as a plain "Draft · Use draft" card; they get the reply's words. The new builds keep their restored cards. The server half reached production with the October 21 release (about 09:15 UTC). Checked again on master on October 22 (evidence in [#2483](https://github.com/WangPantopus/skinny-pantopus/pull/2483)): the 2.0.1 iOS app shows the reply's words, and the new iOS and Android builds show the card after a relaunch.
- [x] Phase 1 follow-up: two limits in what Ask reads, seen on October 20 once Place and Today facts reached the model ([#2481](https://github.com/WangPantopus/skinny-pantopus/pull/2481)). Fixed the same day in [#2486](https://github.com/WangPantopus/skinny-pantopus/pull/2486). A message sent from a screen now says so, and "this", "here" and "these" in it mean that screen, so the Risk starter is answered about the Risk page after any exchange. The weather reaches the model compact, with the next 24 hours in the home's local time. The Place tab's own context gives every section one line (22 of 22), and `get_place_facts` gives a few named sections whole (all the representatives). Checking it found two more, also fixed there: plans today were picked by UTC date, so a 5:30 PM plan in California was missing, and Ask didn't read the Ballot card, so it had no election date. The question set dropped the question about people without Plans, since [#2477](https://github.com/WangPantopus/skinny-pantopus/pull/2477) turned Plans on for everyone. Two follow-ups came out of #2486's review the same day. [#2488](https://github.com/WangPantopus/skinny-pantopus/pull/2488) sends a screen's context once an hour instead of on every turn, and fixes three small bugs the review found. [#2496](https://github.com/WangPantopus/skinny-pantopus/pull/2496) lets Ask on Today see the radon and Ballot cards the screen shows.
- [x] Phase 1 follow-up: playdates in Ask. [#2477](https://github.com/WangPantopus/skinny-pantopus/pull/2477) turned `plans_playdates` on for everyone on October 20, but the assistant still never offered or drafted playdates. Fixed the same day in [#2490](https://github.com/WangPantopus/skinny-pantopus/pull/2490). Ask now follows the flag for each person, and contract rule 5 says so. For people who have it, Ask finds playdates and drafts hosting one: a public place in the place picker's order, never a bar; the kids' ages; the host approves each family; the place stays out of the title. It also drafts a family's ask with how many kids are coming and their ages, the parent staying. The host's request card shows each family's kids. The server's own rules still decide, so people without a verified address get no playdates, and with the flag off nothing changes. The same PR fixed plan change cards: they never warned that a move over 30 minutes asks everyone in "Still coming?", and they could offer fewer spots than the people already in.
- [x] Phase 1 follow-up: deleting a conversation in the app. No app called `DELETE /api/ai/conversations/:id`, so the privacy draft could promise deletion only with the account or on request. Since October 20 the assistant thread's More (…) menu on iOS and Android, and a trash button on the web's assistant page, offer "Delete conversation". After a confirm it deletes every conversation the assistant keeps for the person, including earlier ones that "New chat" or other visits left and no screen shows; deleting only the one on screen would bring the previous one back at the next launch. The phones also clear their finished-card records for those cards. Web [#2510](https://github.com/WangPantopus/skinny-pantopus/pull/2510), iOS [#2511](https://github.com/WangPantopus/skinny-pantopus/pull/2511) and Android [#2512](https://github.com/WangPantopus/skinny-pantopus/pull/2512), each verified against a local backend with the live model. A chat is read only when the person asks about it, only chats they are in, never in the background (`AI_CHAT_READING=off` stops it). It never sends, posts, pays or withdraws. Checked with the live model on a local backend (the question set's groups `mail`, `chats`, `wallet`, `moments`, `schedule`, `guide`: 39 questions) and on the web, the iPhone simulator and the Android emulator.
- [x] Mail and the Wallet stay behind the phones' identity check. Since November 6 the phones show Mail, the Wallet and Payments only after Face ID, a fingerprint or the passcode, so the assistant reads them only when the app says that check has passed (`"identity"` in the chat request). Otherwise it reads nothing and shows the button that opens Mail or the Wallet, where the app asks. The web's pages open with the sign-in, so the web sends it.
- [ ] Connections, the phones: the iPhone and Android apps send `identity` when the check has passed (a few lines each, in a PR of its own). Until those builds ship, the phones' assistant points to Mail and the Wallet instead of reading them; Chats, Moments, the week and the guide already work on today's builds.
- [ ] Yours: the privacy page's section 5 (pantopus.com/privacy) now says the assistant looks up Mail, chats, Moments and the Wallet when asked, that a chat is read only when a participant asks about it, and that the other person's messages are part of what is read. Read it on staging before promoting dev to master. The Anthropic retention confirmation noted there is still open.
- [x] Faster answers (November 14, your request): a latency check, and the fixes that make no answer shorter, vaguer or less correct. Measured on a local backend with the live model: an answer that needs no lookup starts in 0.7 to 1.3 s; one that needs a lookup took 3 to 8 s. [#4083](https://github.com/WangPantopus/skinny-pantopus/pull/4083): the reads before the model is asked run together, the home context is read as the assistant opens, the week's lookup reads less, Claude's cached copy of the instructions is kept warm (about 9 cents a day per kind of app in use; `AI_CHAT_KEEP_WARM=off` stops it), and the `AI chat turn` log line says where each turn's time went. [#4087](https://github.com/WangPantopus/skinny-pantopus/pull/4087): a button under a reply is the reply's own last line instead of a tool call, so an answer with a button takes one request to the model fewer (4.3 s to 3.0 s at the median) and "where is my wallet?" starts in under a second instead of arriving whole after 3 s. Buttons now show under more answers, each opening what the answer is about. What still takes time: every lookup is one more request to the model (1.3 to 2.5 s), and after a lookup Claude Sonnet 5.5's API hands the answer over whole instead of streaming it. Yours: read the `AI chat turn` lines on staging and production for the real numbers (hosted databases add time per call that this Mac doesn't have), say if buttons under most answers are too many, and say if you want lookup answers to stream, which means the model giving its answer through a tool call (a change to how it answers; not built).

## Mail (added October 28)

Decided October 26–27 (section 0). Four sessions (server, iPhone, Android, web) built receiving in parallel on October 27–28, from the [design direction](docs/product/mail-tab-design-direction-2026-10-27.md), the [drawings](https://claude.ai/artifact/Ur16TKZKJM71GEG3eMsXRR) and the [build plan](https://claude.ai/artifact/XNh4cVxnDrS3jMdYW1NPV3) (both private). Receiving is behind `mailbox`, enabled in production at the founder’s direction on November 6. The production API and worker, production web, and iOS 2.0.9 (89) in TestFlight now carry it. The founder subsequently enabled the full Mailbox on November 6: `mailbox`, `mail_extras` and `holiday_cards`, including sending and Christmas cards. See the full-release row below.

- [x] November 6, full Mailbox configuration (founder-authorized): receiving, writing/sending letters and Christmas cards use `mailbox,mail_extras,holiday_cards` on the production API/worker and in `ios-release/PANTOPUS_LAUNCH_FEATURES`. Both production containers are healthy with all three flags. `ios-v2.0.10` pins master `4d435952fc65b13ebd4056278082ec46980a4b48`; the [new TestFlight workflow](https://github.com/WangPantopus/skinny-pantopus/actions/runs/42202274157) records its build and upload status. Vercel production has the same three keys, and they went live with the web deployment of `0e338a984`: signed in on production, the Mail, Christmas card and letter pages open, while Marketplace and Beacons stay hidden. Android has them too: `android-release/PANTOPUS_LAUNCH_FEATURES` is set, and 2.0.12 (31) is a draft on Play's internal track ([run 42314335957](https://github.com/WangPantopus/skinny-pantopus/actions/runs/42314335957), by its upload log; the upload step no longer asks Play for a review hold, [3545](https://github.com/WangPantopus/skinny-pantopus/pull/3545)). `MailEnabled=true` remains active and other launch flags retain their existing settings.
- [x] November 6, production activation and iOS TestFlight upload (founder-authorized). The `ios-release` Actions variable is `PANTOPUS_LAUNCH_FEATURES=mailbox`; tag `ios-v2.0.9` pins the master snapshot at release start, `4d18a207032a848d315c9d4f0dc584fb038e5137`. [The upload](https://github.com/WangPantopus/skinny-pantopus/actions/runs/42195135097) completed and Apple processed version 2.0.9, build 89. Production API/worker have `LAUNCH_FEATURES=mailbox`, Vercel production has `NEXT_PUBLIC_LAUNCH_FEATURES=mailbox`, and the existing seeder stack has `MailEnabled=true`. Only that stack parameter changed; the other activation and capacity settings were preserved. The API and worker passed health checks. Existing mail/flag checks passed (45 tests); the web welcome-letter and Counter persistence check used a real isolated local API/database. A signed simulator Release build also passed. Physical-phone receipt and the full native runtime journey remain separate checks.
- [ ] Yours, the public store release. On November 6 you chose Mail on everywhere, a release soon, and mail notices kept on for App Store users until then. Both builds come from master `76be4468d` with its CI green, and carry the Push switch, the sign-out change, the Nearby "Set an area" fix and all three Mail keys.
  - iPhone: the App Store is on 2.0.7. 2.0.12 (91) is processed and in TestFlight ([run 42314335991](https://github.com/WangPantopus/skinny-pantopus/actions/runs/42314335991)), with the notification extension. Add it as a new version in App Store Connect, submit it for review, then release it.
  - Android: 2.0.12 (31) is a draft on Play's internal track ([run 42314335957](https://github.com/WangPantopus/skinny-pantopus/actions/runs/42314335957)). In the Play Console, check it's under Internal testing and that Publishing overview shows nothing else in review, then roll it out.
  - Neither build has been run on a physical phone yet, so try each on a real phone first.
  - Until then, in test builds with Mail off, standing in for today's store apps (the public binaries themselves weren't run): on iPhone, mail pushes show while the app is in the background, a tap lands on "This feature isn't in the app yet", and the bell's unread count stays over an empty list until "Mark all read". On Android, mail pushes are dropped, so those people never hear about their mail, and the bell's dot stays over an empty list.

## Christmas cards (added October 30)

Decided October 30 (section 0). Christmas cards are built behind `holiday_cards`, which needs `mailbox` on too. The founder enabled both for production and the new iOS release on November 6; the full Mailbox row above records deployment status. With both keys on:
- **The way in.** At the top of their own Mail, people get "Send a Christmas card".
- **Five screens.**
  1. Who it's for: a person found by name, or an email address.
  2. Pick a card: 89 pictures in eight collections (below).
  3. Write on the back: a note of up to 300 letters.
  4. Seal it for the day: with the morning mail, sealed until Christmas, or on Christmas morning.
  5. Look it over: exactly what the other person will get. For a person, that's their Mailbox on the day it comes, the sealed notice if it's sealed, the card sliding out of its envelope, and its back. By email, it's the email, the page its link opens, and the card once they join. "Seal and send" is only here.
- **One card a day.** A card can be taken back until the carrier's 7 a.m. pickup, which frees the day.
- **Sent.** A Sent drawer in Mail lists the cards you sent, newest first. Cards still waiting for the 7 a.m. pickup come first, where you can take them back, then the rest by the day you sent them, each saying where it is now. Sent never says whether a card was opened. A card's page shows the card with your note and its trail. While a card waits for the pickup, the mailbox's flag is up. Sent needs only `mailbox`; the flag and everything about sending need `holiday_cards` too.
- **Receiving.** The card comes as a red envelope. A sealed one stays shut, with its seal and the days left, until 7 a.m. on December 25 where the other person lives. Opening it slides the card out; turning it over shows the note.
- **Christmas morning.** The 7 a.m. mail notice also counts the sealed cards that open that morning.

Two follow-ups came from the Android build. A phone build from before cards can't mark a sealed card read, so it still opens from its envelope on Christmas morning ([#3015](https://github.com/WangPantopus/skinny-pantopus/pull/3015)). A card to an email address starts "Dear friend," ([#3016](https://github.com/WangPantopus/skinny-pantopus/pull/3016)). There are no new tables and no migration.   - the Christmas-morning notice, captured locally: "A sealed card can be opened now."

And also for each picture I wonder if you could help pick a Bible verse to it to put in the Mail?", then "Let's also not forget the John 3:16" and "ok let's go, full scripts please"): "For the faithful, and anyone who loves the Christmas story." Later that day you renamed it Christmas with Jesus Christ and moved it to the front, before Christmas as a Kid. It is a server-side change, so the website, iPhone and Android apps show it without an app update once the backend is released.
  - **The cards.** Nine of your nativity pictures, numbered 59 to 67, in the order of the story. Each has its whole verse from the King James Version printed under the painting: Isaiah 9:6, Luke 2:7, John 3:16, Luke 2:10, Luke 2:14, Matthew 2:2, Matthew 2:11, Matthew 2:14 and John 8:12. The handwritten line under the picture is the verse's reference, such as "John 3:16 (KJV)".
  - **Why the verse is in the picture.** Today's apps print that handwritten line on one line that shrinks to fit, so a whole verse there would be too small to read. Printed in the picture, a verse takes up to three lines and reads easily on the smallest phones. Screen readers say the reference, not the verse.
  - **Left out.** The two crucifixion scenes and the risen Christ, which are Easter pictures, and one shepherds picture that nearly repeats another.
  - **Cloths.** They follow the card table's rule, except dusk for the two night skies and wine for Mary holding the baby and for the Magi's gifts.
  - **Shipping.** The website pictures went first ([#3811](https://github.com/WangPantopus/skinny-pantopus/pull/3811), on pantopus.com from 4:35 a.m. Pacific on November 8). Then the server ([#3812](https://github.com/WangPantopus/skinny-pantopus/pull/3812)), which lists the collection for everyone once its backend release cuts over. No app changes and no store builds.
  - **The ESV.** You asked for the ESV. Crossway's rules need their written permission for greeting cards, so the cards use the King James Version, which is free to use. If Crossway agrees, ESV copies can be added as new pictures and swapped in, and cards already sent keep theirs.

**The card on the table (added November 6).** Built November 6–7 behind `card_table`, which is off, from [the design folder](docs/product/christmas-card-table/README.md): one session for the server and web, one for the iPhone and the Duo, and one for Android. With the key on, on all three apps:
- **Full screen, on a table.** Opening a card hides the app's bars. The red envelope with its gold wax seal lies on a cloth whose colour suits the picture, in window light that moves as the phone tilts. People turn the envelope over, lift the flap and pull the card out by hand, or use the buttons. A swipe turns the card over to the note.
- **Sealed cards** stay shut until 7 a.m. on Christmas morning, showing the seal and the days left.
- **The iPhone Duo:** the card is a folded card, with the picture on the left page, the note on the right and the crease on the hinge. It opens with the phone and keeps its place through every fold and turn.
- **Mail:** the card row in the Mailbox uses the new paper at the same size. Look it over gains "Open it the way they will", and a card's page in Sent gains "See it the way they did".
- **Share:** Story, Post and link-preview pictures, then Instagram, Facebook, email, Copy link and Save. The note and the name stay off unless "Show the note" is on, and then only the first name shows. An address never shows. A sealed card shares only its sealed envelope.
- **Send by link,** a third choice on "Who it's for". One link is for one person: the first to open it keeps the card and joins to read it. It counts as that day's card, can be taken back until it's opened, and lapses after 90 days. Sent shows each state, with Copy link again.
- **Public card pages** (`/cards`, and one page per picture): people arriving from a shared link see the card and "Send this card to someone". Sign-ups from them credit the person who shared it.
