**The page a card's link opens (added November 16).** You asked for more sign-ups from cards, and decided that the page may now show the card's front before the person joins: the stock picture the sender picked, never the note, signature, photo or voice.

- [x] The front before sign-up: `GET /api/mailbox/claim/:mailId` answers `card.front` (the picture's id, its printed caption, paper and cloth, from the season's list), null while the card is sealed until Christmas. The web page draws the card lying on its open envelope, as `/cards/<slug>` does, and keeps the shut envelope and "sealed until Christmas morning" for a sealed card; the link's preview in chat apps stays the sealed envelope. Look it over on the web shows the same page (`link_page.front`). Checked in a browser against a local server and database: an unsealed card by email showed its front, a sealed one the shut envelope.
- [x] Opening it with one code, no password: on the page, "Email me a code" sends a 6-digit code to the address the card (or a letter by email) was sent to, shown masked as before; a card by link asks for their email first. Entering it signs them in, making a free account when the address has none (no password or name; the web asks "What should we call you?" as for any nameless account), puts the card in their Mailbox and opens it. The page's line says that continuing accepts the Terms of Service and Privacy Policy, as the sign-up form's checkbox does. "Sign in another way" keeps password, Apple and Google. Before, a new person needed about 8 steps: Create a free account, a form of 5 fields and the terms box, the confirmation email, back to sign in (email and password again, unless the same tab was still open), back to the page and "Open your card". Now: "Email me a code", the code from the email, "Open your card".
  - The code is Supabase Auth's own email code (`admin.generateLink`, checked with `verifyOtp`), sent through our SMTP: `POST /api/mailbox/claim/:mailId/code` sends it, `POST /api/users/mail-code` checks it and signs in, and the existing `POST /api/mailbox/claim/:mailId` claims it with its usual rule (the address must be the account's confirmed one). The claim token alone signs no one in. A code works 10 minutes; each new code replaces the last; wrong, used, replaced and expired codes are refused in words. Limits: sending 10 per 15 minutes per network, then, only for a link that works, one per 30 seconds and 5 an hour per letter and per address; checking 10 wrong per 15 minutes per network, then, only for a link that works, 6 wrong an hour per letter and per address. The answer never says whether the address had an account.
  - A code stops working after 5 tries (founder, November 16: the 5th wrong one stops it, even if the right digits come next; the page says so and "Send a new code" starts the count again). The count is per address, across every link and network, kept in `AuthSecurityEvent` rows (`mail_code_sent`, found by a keyed hash of the address and a new partial index, and `mail_code_try`; left out of the person's security activity), so it holds across restarts and processes. Stopping replaces the address's codes in Supabase Auth with ones nobody is sent, so the old code fails there too; it signs nobody out, changes no password, and answers the same whether or not the address has an account. It also replaces a password-reset link sent to that address in the meantime. Each new code also replaces an unconfirmed address's earlier one.
  - An address whose account was never confirmed (an unused code, or a sign-up form nobody confirmed) gets a fresh random password when its code is used (even one entered too late, which still confirms the address), so a password set by someone who never proved the address stops working; "Forgot password" sets a real one. And a normal sign-up for an address that was sent a code it never used now keeps its password (before, the unused code's Auth user swallowed it).
  - A letter sent by text keeps Sign in / Create a free account: a code by text would need Supabase Auth's phone provider (an SMS account), which is yours to decide.
- [ ] The phones' Look it over still draws the link's page as the shut envelope for every card (iOS `CardTableLinkPagePicture` and `HolidayCardLinkPagePicture`, Android `LinkPageOnTable` and `LinkPagePicture`): they need to draw `link_page.front` when it's there, and "Email me a code" in place of Sign in / Create a free account.

## The travelling card (added November 21)

Approved by you on November 21 (the plan and its six decisions are in [docs/product/travelling-card-2026-11-21](docs/product/travelling-card-2026-11-21/README.md)). Whoever receives a Christmas card can pass the same card on, free, with a note of their own, to up to two people. The envelope collects a postmark in every town, a public page (`/cards/journey/<id>`) shows the journey on a US map with towns and dates only, and everyone who held the card hears when it reaches a town. It is behind one new server-only switch, `card_travel`, which needs `mailbox`, `holiday_cards` and `card_table`.

- [x] **On `dev` and staging since November 21, 11:19 a.m. Pacific**, with `card_travel` on in staging (staging's `.env.staging` was backed up as `.env.staging.before-card-travel-20261121T191829Z` and the running image redeployed by Rollback Backend run 44940235453).
  - Server: [#4539](https://github.com/WangPantopus/skinny-pantopus/pull/4539), [#4543](https://github.com/WangPantopus/skinny-pantopus/pull/4543) and the hardening in [#4573](https://github.com/WangPantopus/skinny-pantopus/pull/4573). No new table; one additive index (`mail_card_travel_journey_idx`). The towns come from the Census lists bundled with the server (no outside service).
  - Web: "Let it travel" and "Where does it start?" ([#4547](https://github.com/WangPantopus/skinny-pantopus/pull/4547)), the postmarks ([#4548](https://github.com/WangPantopus/skinny-pantopus/pull/4548)), "Pass it on" ([#4549](https://github.com/WangPantopus/skinny-pantopus/pull/4549)), the journey page and its picture ([#4551](https://github.com/WangPantopus/skinny-pantopus/pull/4551)), the Sent line and the notice ([#4552](https://github.com/WangPantopus/skinny-pantopus/pull/4552)), sharing and the card link page ([#4553](https://github.com/WangPantopus/skinny-pantopus/pull/4553)), the goal line on `/cards` ([#4554](https://github.com/WangPantopus/skinny-pantopus/pull/4554)).

The phones open the journey page in the app (D6).

## Profiles (added November 1)

Decided November 1 (section 0). Four sessions built it in parallel on November 1, one each for the server, iPhone, Android and web, from the drawings and the build plan. There is no on/off key. The server is in production and the web has the new profiles now; phones get them with the next store builds.

- **Someone else's profile.** The header, then one button for where you stand: Connect, Requested, Accept or Connected. The Connect sheet takes an optional note, which arrives in their notification, or lets you just follow their posts.
  - "You and Rowan" shows up to two lines of common ground: a plan you're both in, a Support Train you both help with, people you both know, or skills you share.
  - Then up to three prompts, up to three pinned posts, and Posts and About.
  - About adds "Around the neighborhood": the Support Trains they helped, the plans they hosted, and how often neighbors marked their posts helpful.
  - A brand-new neighbor's profile says "Jordan just joined", with Say hi.
- **Your own profile.** The same page, with Edit profile and Preview as on the first screen. Preview shows exactly what each audience gets: anyone with the link, neighbors, or your connections. Anyone with the link sees no posts ("Posts are for neighbors").
- **The editor.** It has two parts, "Neighbors see this" and "Only you see this", with a preview card that updates as you type. The name and photo neighbors see moved into it from Privacy. Prompts and pins are edited here too.

- [x] Server: what you have in common, the "Around the neighborhood" counts and Preview as ([#3098](https://github.com/WangPantopus/skinny-pantopus/pull/3098)); prompts and profile pins ([#3099](https://github.com/WangPantopus/skinny-pantopus/pull/3099)). Both are in production since the morning of November 1. Deploy Backend applied both migrations, `20261101103700_user_profile_prompts` and `20261101103800_post_profile_pinned_at`. Block in the app now also hides a person's posts on their profile, as it already did in the feeds.
- [x] Web: [#3100](https://github.com/WangPantopus/skinny-pantopus/pull/3100), [#3101](https://github.com/WangPantopus/skinny-pantopus/pull/3101), [#3106](https://github.com/WangPantopus/skinny-pantopus/pull/3106), [#3108](https://github.com/WangPantopus/skinny-pantopus/pull/3108), [#3112](https://github.com/WangPantopus/skinny-pantopus/pull/3112). iPhone: [#3107](https://github.com/WangPantopus/skinny-pantopus/pull/3107), [#3109](https://github.com/WangPantopus/skinny-pantopus/pull/3109), [#3110](https://github.com/WangPantopus/skinny-pantopus/pull/3110), [#3111](https://github.com/WangPantopus/skinny-pantopus/pull/3111), [#3115](https://github.com/WangPantopus/skinny-pantopus/pull/3115). ## Home Money Check (added November 9)

Tells a verified homeowner whether their home costs more than it should, from records Pantopus holds. The plan is the [build plan](https://claude.ai/artifact/YB4JHYrY5WzjyQSxZfx5gU): five steps, the founder's own steps and the sizing of the other four moves.

**Made by the founder on November 9.**

- [x] Build the Home Money Check, starting with steps 1 and 2 ("Yes, start 1 and 2"), then: "keep going until you finish all of the work related to the improvements, no need to stop, we want to launch it", handing off to a new session when one fills up.
- [x] November 10, for the appeal packet ("Yes, go ahead" to these): it costs $29, flat, sold on the web only at first; the PDF, and only the PDF, lists the similar homes by parcel number, living area, year built and county value, with no street address and no owner's name; one new table records purchases; no Today card for now.

**Built (each off until a county is switched on).**

- [x] Similar homes (server): a confirmed owner's value card shows the county's value per square foot beside up to ten similar houses on the street; an owner who hasn't verified yet gets one true line on the locked Your home card. [#3837](https://github.com/WangPantopus/skinny-pantopus/pull/3837), merged November 10 and released; checked on web, iPhone and Android.
- [x] The Property tax check card on the money page, web, iPhone and Android, sent by the server as `money_cards` so a later check needs no app release. [#3839](https://github.com/WangPantopus/skinny-pantopus/pull/3839), merged November 10; checked on web, iPhone and Android. The phones show it from the first store build cut after that; until then they show the same figures in the value card on Your home.
- [x] A yearly notice when the county values a home above similar homes, and how the state reviews a value (Washington, Texas, Florida). [#3840](https://github.com/WangPantopus/skinny-pantopus/pull/3840). The weekly job is registered beside the rate watch; whether the production worker runs these weekly jobs wasn't visible from here.
- [x] The home insurance check (step 5). Send a photo of the policy's renewal page to Pantopus AI on a phone: the reminder it drafts now also keeps the premium for the term, the term before when the page prints it, the day the term starts and the insurer's name, and never the policy number. The money page then shows a Home insurance card with the two terms side by side (web, iPhone and Android, with no app release), and a policy's first reminder comes 45 days before it ends. This card needs no county: it shows for any confirmed owner who saved a policy. An owner with no policy on file sees a short starting card only where a county is switched on. [#3843](https://github.com/WangPantopus/skinny-pantopus/pull/3843), merged November 10; checked on web, iPhone and Android with made-up policy pages.
- [x] The appeal packet's document (step 4, first part), off behind its own switch. For a confirmed owner whose home the county values above similar homes: an "Appeal packet" card on the money page that opens a web page, and a two-page PDF to file oneself (the home in the county's records, the comparison, how the state reviews a value with its official page, the steps, a blank worksheet). It names no other home, takes no payment yet and says on every page that Pantopus doesn't file for anyone. [#3844](https://github.com/WangPantopus/skinny-pantopus/pull/3844); checked on the web (card, page, download) and the card on iPhone and Android.
- [x] Buying the appeal packet (step 4, second part), off with it. The packet's web page sells it for $29 through Stripe's checkout page, once for a home and a county value year, and the owner can download it again. The PDF now lists the similar homes by parcel number, living area, year built and county value; the page and the card still show only the count, the typical figure and the range. A refund or dispute closes the download. A new table, `AppealPacketPurchase`, holds one row per purchase (added to production's database by the release that carries this). [#3858](https://github.com/WangPantopus/skinny-pantopus/pull/3858); checked on the web in Stripe's test mode (pay, come back, download, sign in again, refund), and the card's price line on iPhone and Android.

**Measured on live releases:** Clark County, WA: 156,087 houses, 64% get a comparison, 19% of those above similar homes. Dallas County, TX: 561,002 houses, 85%, 23% above. Alachua County, FL: 71,134 houses, 62%, 18% above.

**Yours to do.**

- [ ] Switch it on for a county, staging first: `gh workflow run property-index.yml -f action=similar-homes -f county=53011 -f mode=live -f target=staging`, then `-f target=production`. `-f mode=off` takes it away within about a minute.
- [ ] Show the locked line and the card to about twenty homeowners who have never heard of Pantopus before the packet is built (the plan's test).
- [ ] Switch the packet on for a county only after the lawyer's read and the store-rule check below, because it takes real money at once: `gh workflow run property-index.yml -f action=appeal-packet -f county=53011 -f mode=live -f target=staging`. It shows only where similar homes are on too.
- [ ] Know before you switch the packet on: real Stripe payments are already on in production (configured October 26, section 0), so the packet is sold for a real $29 from the moment a county goes on the `appeal_packet` list in production. There is no separate payments step. The live webhook already sends the three events the packet uses (`checkout.session.completed`, `charge.refunded`, `charge.dispute.created`, per the release checklist's P11 list). A server without a live key (staging, a test rig) says "The packet is not on sale yet. It will cost $29." unless it sets `APPEAL_PACKET_SANDBOX=true`, which sells it for test money.
- [ ] Before the packet is switched on, check Apple's and Google's current rules on an app linking to a web page that sells a digital item: the card on the phones opens the packet's web page. If you'd rather the phones not link there, say so and the card can leave its link off for them.
- [ ] Have a lawyer read the card's and the packet's wording, and say whether a refund rule should be printed. Every word is in `backend/services/appealPacket.js`.
- [ ] Choose an insurance quote partner. The insurance check is built and names no insurer and no partner; a "get quotes" link waits for this.

**Not built yet.**

- [ ] A Today card: set aside on November 10. The apps accept only the Today kinds they know, so it needs iPhone and Android code and store builds; the yearly notice already tells the owner.
- [ ] Selling the packet inside the phone apps (it is on the web only).

## Background backlog

The September acceptance backlog ([REMAINING_WORK](docs/REMAINING_WORK_2026-09-11.md)) is background; its row IDs are handy names, not a process. The launch streams verify their areas end to end, and L4 runs the final release-candidate sweep on iOS, Android and the web before the pilot.

- [x] Support Train page during a sign-in outage: [#2550](https://github.com/WangPantopus/skinny-pantopus/pull/2550). An unavailable token check or failed Group chat membership read now returns the existing retryable 503. Guest reservations also wait when a supplied token cannot be checked. iOS gives cached pages a recipient-details retry button and disables sign-up until those details reload. Verified outage and recovery in the web app, iOS simulator and Android emulator against a local backend and database; private details stay hidden during the outage and return after retry. The production migration and API/worker deploy steps succeeded in [run 39592735125](https://github.com/WangPantopus/skinny-pantopus/actions/runs/39592735125), after master CI passed. Hosted user-flow verification and an iOS store build containing the fix remain open.

- [x] October 21, dependency security: [#2551](https://github.com/WangPantopus/skinny-pantopus/pull/2551) updates the existing `follow-redirects` override to 1.16.1. The production dependency audit reports no known vulnerabilities. Existing web checks passed, and signed-in navigation, notifications, mark-all-read and reload persistence passed in the local web app. PR and master CI passed.

- [x] October 21, Android association source: the existing generator now includes `app.pantopus.android` with the current Play app-signing SHA-256 from Play Console's Digital Asset Links snippet and both public certificates from its previous quantum-ready signing key. The legacy `com.pantopus.app` entry is preserved. No debug certificate or secret key is included. Production hosting and a Play-installed device verification remain separate release checks.

## Around you (added November 14)

Decided November 14 (section 0). Built that day and checked in a browser, the iPhone simulator and the Android emulator against a local backend and database, with the project's own fetcher run once against the live feeds (301 real items for Clark County and Portland metro).

- [x] Server ([#4094](https://github.com/WangPantopus/skinny-pantopus/pull/4094) into `dev`, [#4095](https://github.com/WangPantopus/skinny-pantopus/pull/4095) into `master`): `backend/services/aroundYouService.js` reads the list the content fetcher already fills every two hours and answers sections (Alerts, Headlines, Events, Around town, This season, On this day) for the content regions that cover the place. Four routes, one beside each Today: a Home, a saved address, a city point, and the web's briefing. With the switch on, the Pulse list and map leave out the curator account's posts.
- [x] Web, iPhone and Android: the switch on Today and the Around you page. A headline opens the story at its outlet (a new tab on the web, the in-app browser on the phones). At home stays as it was, and the switch is absent where the server has nothing to show.
- [x] What the checks showed: real headlines with outlet names (The Columbian, OPB, KGW, and the outlet Google News names) for a Vancouver, WA home, for a saved-city Today after "Use my location", and on the web's Today; a point in Miami (no region) answers "nothing to show"; signed out 401, someone else's home 403, someone else's saved place 404; a post marked as the curator's leaves the Pulse list and map with the switch on and returns with it off; a relaunch keeps Today working and opens on At home.
- Left out on purpose: local sports (the regions' sports sources are mostly league-wide feeds and team searches; a memorabilia listing and a trade elsewhere came up in the check). They still feed the Pulse Sports lane.
- The time under a headline is when Pantopus first saw the story (the fetcher runs every two hours), not the outlet's publishing time, which the list does not keep.
- A word list keeps crime, obituaries and party politics off the page (the fetcher's own, plus a second pass on the server). A charged story can still get through as the outlet's own headline.
- [ ] Not checked anywhere: the hosted staging and production sites, real phones, a real iPhone Duo (its layouts were checked on Apple's Duo simulator, below), a screen reader speaking aloud, and an area whose only region was made automatically when a home was added.
- [ ] Yours: staging shows Around you once the web and the store builds carry it. Production's `AROUND_YOU` has been on since November 14 ([the production checklist](docs/release/prod-config-checklist.md)); the posts reach production when you promote `dev` to `master`, and the first person to open Around you there makes that area's posts. Run `sam deploy --config-env prod` (and `--config-env staging`) from `pantopus-seeder/deploy` to stop the posting job: the template now has `SeederPostingEnabled`, off by default, so the fetcher keeps running and the poster stops (it has failed in production since October 26, when the Anthropic account behind it ran out of credit, and its alarm has fired three times a day since). Hold that deploy if you decide the next step should keep the rewritten posts. The staging posting schedule was left running for the same reason; its posts no longer show in Pulse. Cut new iPhone and Android store builds; phones show none of this until then.
- [x] Each Around you item is a real post ([#4102](https://github.com/WangPantopus/skinny-pantopus/pull/4102), into `dev`). The server makes one post for each headline it shows, by the region's curator account: the outlet's headline as written, the outlet's name and the link; no rewriting, no photo, no Claude call. One headline makes one post however many people open the page at once, and a post a moderator removes stays removed. Nothing is made while `AROUND_YOU` is off.
- [x] Pictures (November 15, [#4122](https://github.com/WangPantopus/skinny-pantopus/pull/4122)): a post carries the pictures the fetcher kept from the outlet's feed for that story, as the curator's Pulse posts did. They are links to the outlet's own files (secure links to images only; a video or an insecure link is left out), so a picture the outlet takes down stops showing, and the outlet's server sees the request of each person who views it. Posts made before this get their pictures the next time someone opens Around you there. In the test data 73 of 78 local news stories have one; stories that come through Google News and the seasonal tip have none. Checked through the API, in a browser, on the iPhone simulator and on the Android emulator: a post with one picture, with four and with none, on the card and on the post's page, and the viewer opening and closing.
- [x] A picture that can't load (November 15, [#4134](https://github.com/WangPantopus/skinny-pantopus/pull/4134)): the phone checks found that a dead picture link left a gray box with a warning mark where the picture would be. Now, on a post that names its outlet, a picture that fails to load while the device is online is left out: the card and the post's page lay out the pictures that did load (four with one dead become the three-picture layout), the viewer pages through those, and a post whose pictures all fail shows no picture area. People's own uploads keep the mark. Web, iPhone and Android. On the web, three pictures also fill their grid now: the first one left the quarter under it empty on any three-picture post.
- [x] iPhone Duo (November 15, [#4134](https://github.com/WangPantopus/skinny-pantopus/pull/4134), iPhone only). Folded: the phone's page. Open and held level, flat or half folded like a book: the list is the page left of the hinge, under Today's title and the switch; the post you tap opens on the page right of it at the screen's full height, its card tinted in the list; before one is picked that page says "Pick a post" (it never opens one by itself, because opening a post counts a view). Open and upright: the list and the post's page are one centered column, about a phone and a third wide. At home is as it was in every posture. A fold, an unfold or a turn moves the open post between the pushed page and the page beside the list once the move is over, and keeps a reply being typed and its keyboard, an open picture viewer, the story's browser sheet and the quote composer. One thing it does not keep: after a fold or a turn the viewer shows the picture it was opened on again, not the one paged to (three tries at keeping the page did not hold on the simulator and were taken out).
- [x] With it, on every iPhone: a card's "Post options" and report menus open at that card's own button (they were attached to the whole page, and on the open Duo floated at the top of it); an Around you card's ↻ opens the Repost chooser; a reply's text shows every line (beside the list it was cut to one line with "…"; what limited it there was not found, so the reply text now takes its lines whatever it is offered).
- [x] What the Duo checks showed (Apple's iPhone Duo simulator on iOS 27.1, a local backend and database, real fetched headlines): every posture above in light and dark; the list, the post's page with its pictures, four levels of nested replies and "Replying to", the reaction picker, a card's options and report menus, the toast, the Repost chooser and the quote composer, the picture viewer and the story sheet; twenty posture changes in a row with a reply typed and the keyboard up, the text and the keyboard kept each time; folds and turns with the viewer, the story sheet and the quote composer open. A regular iPhone shows the list, the pictures, the post's page, Back and swipe back as before.
- Left for other work, seen on the open Duo and the same in Pulse: the post page's own "Post options" menu opens beside the page instead of at its button, and the "Repost with your words" composer runs the screen's whole width. Seen on iPhone and Android, not changed: the same curator post is labelled "Announce" on its card and "Local update" on its page. On the web, a quoted Around you post whose first picture is dead still says "Image unavailable" in the small quoted card.
- [x] Web, iPhone and Android draw them with the card Pulse uses: emoji reactions, Save, Repost, the post menu (Share, Report), and Reply. A tap opens the post's own page from Today, at once from the card's copy, with its replies. "Read at The Columbian" under the headline opens the story (a new tab on the web, the in-app browser on iPhone, a browser tab on Android), on the card and on the post's page.
- [x] Reply threads nest on the phones as they already did on the web: a reply sits under the comment it answers, one step further in each time (four steps at most). This is the post page every Pulse post uses, so Pulse threads read the same way.
- [x] Where these posts show: only on Today's Around you, on their own page, and in a person's saved posts. They are never in the Pulse nearby list or on its map, whatever the switch says. A repost shows the post in Pulse's Connections list, to the reposter and their connections ("<name> reposted"), as you asked on November 14; it does not lift the post into the Nearby list, which only lifts posts the area already holds.
- [x] What the checks showed (local backend and database, real fetched headlines): one account's reaction and replies show to a second account on the web, the iPhone simulator and the Android emulator; a four-level thread written across web and iPhone reads as one thread on all three; the article opens from the card and from the post's page; a relaunch keeps everything; a reposted post is in the reposter's connection's Connections list and in nobody's nearby list or map; with `AROUND_YOU=0` no post is made and Today shows no switch.
- The sections keep their names (Headlines, Events, Around town, This season, On this day), and the seasonal tip and "On this day" are posts too. "die" joined the word list (a "woman, 2 sons die" headline had got through and would now collect reactions).
- [x] Repost with a quote: built for every Pulse post by the Pulse post-buttons work ([#4108](https://github.com/WangPantopus/skinny-pantopus/pull/4108) server, the web half, [#4128](https://github.com/WangPantopus/skinny-pantopus/pull/4128) iPhone, [#4136](https://github.com/WangPantopus/skinny-pantopus/pull/4136) Android, all on `dev`). A person's post "with your words" about an Around you headline is their own post, sent to the audience they pick, with the headline and "Read at <outlet>" attached, so local news reaches Pulse through people, never through the curator account. An Around you card has the chooser on the web (checked in a browser) and, since [#4134](https://github.com/WangPantopus/skinny-pantopus/pull/4134), on iPhone (checked on the simulators): ↻ opens "Repost" or "Repost with your words", and the second opens the Pulse composer with the headline attached. On Android since November 15 too ([#4140](https://github.com/WangPantopus/skinny-pantopus/pull/4140); Around you's cards had kept the plain toggle when #4136 landed): checked on the emulator against a local backend, Undo repost and Repost from the chooser with their toasts and the card's "You reposted" line, "Repost with your words" posting to Connections with the headline and its picture as the quoted card (it lists in Pulse → Connections above the reposted headline), and the repost kept after a relaunch. One gap for that work: the attached headline card shows no "Read at <outlet>" link yet, though the server sends it.

## Around you: the everywhere layer (added November 24)

Decided November 24: you asked what a person in a place with few users would see (Miami; Leavenworth, WA) and chose "the everywhere layer" first. Built in three pull requests into `dev`, each checked against a local backend and database with the live feeds, then in a browser, on the iPhone simulator and on the Android emulator (two accounts reacting and replying, a relaunch). Server and web admin only: no app code changed and no store build is needed. A fourth piece, sources found for a new county and a review page for them, is built separately.

- [x] The feeds job ([#4790](https://github.com/WangPantopus/skinny-pantopus/pull/4790)): every hour the backend worker reads public feeds that work for any US address and adds what is near each Around you place to the list Around you reads: wildfires (National Interagency Fire Center: within 50 miles, under 90% contained, changed in the last three days; the link opens the fire's InciWeb page or the AirNow Fire and Smoke Map), federal disaster declarations for the place's county (FEMA), state road projects (the federal work-zone feeds that need no key) and calendar files. One fire or project keeps one post as its numbers change, with its reactions and replies. Outside requests use the place's centre rounded to about a kilometre.
- [x] The page ([#4796](https://github.com/WangPantopus/skinny-pantopus/pull/4796)): Alerts · Roads · Headlines · Events · Around town · Across <your state> · This season · On this day · On watch. Google's news search is off the whole page and no longer fetched for local news or "around town" (the sports searches stay for Pulse), and a new place no longer gets one; one switch in the code brings it back to the page. A story naming the place's city or county comes first; free outlets come before subscription ones, whose link reads "Read at <outlet> (subscription)"; each story shows the outlet's own short summary; letters, opinion and commentary pieces are left out. "Across Washington" shows the state outlet's stories about what touches a home (taxes, insurance, housing, rent, utilities, roads, schools, wildfire, flood). A quiet place gets one "On watch" card a day saying what was checked and found clear. Opens are counted (at most once an hour per person), and `/app/admin/funnel` shows Around you for the last 7 and 30 days: people who opened it, and reactions, replies, saves and reposts on its posts.
- [x] Clark County and Portland sources ([#4798](https://github.com/WangPantopus/skinny-pantopus/pull/4798)): The Columbian's local feed, Clark County Today, the City of Vancouver's news, the Washington State Standard and the Oregon Capital Chronicle, the Post-Record kept a whole week, and three calendars (Camas Public Library, Downtown Camas Association, City of Washougal). The City of Vancouver publishes no calendar file.
- What it gives on a calm day is little: these feeds speak when something happens. On November 24 a Leavenworth address showed the chamber's events or, without them, the On watch card; Miami showed four Florida road projects and no Google rows; an address beside the White Oak Flat fire in Virginia showed it with its acres, containment and distance.
- [ ] Not checked anywhere: the hosted staging and production sites, real phones, KGW's and OregonLive's paywalls (they refuse automated readers, so they are not marked), Washington's road closures and pass conditions (below).
- [ ] Yours:
  1. Washington's road code: at https://wsdot.wa.gov/traffic/api/ enter an email; the state sends a free access code for closures and mountain pass conditions. Tell the design session when you have it: the value goes in the hosted staging and production environments as `WSDOT_ACCESS_CODE`, and a small follow-up reads it.
  2. Promote `dev` to `master` when staging's Around you looks right.
  3. Production only, if new counties should get their own sources there: set `AROUND_YOU_SOURCE_FINDER=1` on the production backend, and make sure the backend's Anthropic key has credit. Nothing shows until someone approves a proposed source on `/app/admin/sources`.
  4. Later, not in this build: local emergency alerts through FEMA need a signed agreement with FEMA.

## Pulse reposts and repost with your words (added November 15)

Decided November 14 (section 0). Built November 14 and 15 and checked in a browser, on an iPhone simulator and on an Android emulator against a local backend and database, with three test accounts acting on each other's posts across the three apps.

- [x] Server ([#4108](https://github.com/WangPantopus/skinny-pantopus/pull/4108), on `dev` and staging): Nearby and Connections list a post at the time you or one of your connections reposted it and say who (`reposted_by`, "You" first); a profile's posts can include reposts (`include_reposts=true`, for the owner and their connections); `GET /api/posts/reposts`; `DELETE /api/posts/hide/:id`; "Not helpful here" also hides the post for the person who flagged it; a post can be about another post (`quotedPostId`, the one new column `Post.quoted_post_id`), returned as `quoted_post` on every list and the post page, or "not available" when the original is gone or the reader may not see it. 57 live checks of the routes pass.
- [x] Web ([#4110](https://github.com/WangPantopus/skinny-pantopus/pull/4110), [#4119](https://github.com/WangPantopus/skinny-pantopus/pull/4119)), iPhone ([#4128](https://github.com/WangPantopus/skinny-pantopus/pull/4128)) and Android ([#4136](https://github.com/WangPantopus/skinny-pantopus/pull/4136)): the Repost chooser from ↻ and from the ⋯ menu, the "reposted" line changing at once, the composer for "Repost with your words" (one box, "Your words", on the phones; no headline), the quoted card on cards, post pages and profiles, the Reposts tab, "Saved." with View, Undo for Hide and for "Not helpful here".
- [x] What the checks showed: a repost made on the iPhone shows on the web and on Android with the reposter's name, first in Nearby; a quote written on the web shows on the iPhone with its card, and one written on Android beside one written on the iPhone; a stranger nearby sees no change; a muted connection's reposts stop lifting and naming; Undo puts a hidden card back where it was; everything is kept after a relaunch.
- [ ] iPhone follow-up ([#4137](https://github.com/WangPantopus/skinny-pantopus/pull/4137), open when this was written): the audience step of a quote keeps the title "Repost with your words", plus what a second pass on the simulator finds (the post page's chooser, profile cards, a quote whose original is gone, large text).
- Found and fixed on the way: the phones' composer asked a quote for a headline; the iPhone chooser floated over the tab bar (it now points at ↻); the Android repost toast ran under the compose button (it now wraps).
- "Posts 5" on your own web profile counts your reposts, while "See all 4 posts" under it counts the posts you wrote (it opens My posts).
- [ ] Not checked anywhere: the hosted staging and production sites, real phones, the iPhone Duo layout, a screen reader speaking aloud, tablets.

## Plans for the holidays (added November 15)

Decided November 15 (section 0). Design, contract and build plan: [docs/product/plans-holidays](docs/product/plans-holidays/README.md); what was handed over and how the build ran: [the hand-off](docs/product/plans-holidays/09-handoff.md). **Where it stands on November 17: everything discussed and designed is built and on `dev`** on the server, the web, the iPhone and Android: the people track (answers without an account, the open link, the bring list, occasions), the business track (deals, paying for a spot with the group price, the bill at the place with tips, the tip afterwards) and the six extras (the same people again, chip in, find a date, the gift exchange, photos afterwards, one person pays for the group). Each piece was checked by its own session in the real app from both people's sides, after a relaunch and with its switch off; the two phones were then checked together on the people track, on the business track in the payment company's test mode with the payout, and on each of the six extras; every money amount in the payments document and the extras' "Done when" was seen to the cent on a test setup. The items below say, piece by piece, what was seen and what was not. Every switch is off except guests answering in a browser on staging; staging's server has all of it since 6:47 AM Pacific on November 17 (`dev` 96a1c994da), untried there; production has none of it. Nothing here was checked on a physical phone or with real money. **What only you can do is the list at the end of this section.**

- [ ] Yours, a one-minute look: in the live Stripe dashboard, open a recent card payment and see whether "Fee" shows an amount (about 2.9% plus 30 cents) or $0. The test account reports $0 on every charge. Where a business pays the card fee (its own guests, bills, tips), the build uses the reported fee when there is one and otherwise 2.9% plus 30 cents; if live also shows $0, that standard rate is what a business pays unless you want another rule.
- [ ] Yours before any paid step is switched on for real people ([build plan, section 3](docs/product/plans-holidays/04-build-plan.md#3-the-business-track-and-its-gates)): the by-hand trial with two or three local businesses, the lawyer's read of the terms, a re-read of both stores' payment rules, the accountant's answer on Washington sales tax, and one real payment end to end on staging. Building does not wait for these; switching on does. The accountant's or the lawyer's answer may change the bill at the place after it is built.

Two of the five were fixed the same day ([#4259](https://github.com/WangPantopus/skinny-pantopus/pull/4259)): a person who took a spot and leaves before the deadline now sends that spot's price back to the buyer, as its own refund, and two such refunds no longer merge; its session saw $144.00 then $18.00 more come back and the business paid out on what stayed, to the cent. The same change makes an unsent refund loud in the server's error log, and it re-checked the amounts for a guest Pantopus brought after the refund fix ($15.30, $11.90 and $42.83 to the business). The last three were fixed that afternoon ([#4261](https://github.com/WangPantopus/skinny-pantopus/pull/4261)): a buyer the business removes gets back their own spot and the untaken ones while the people they paid for stay in, paid for ($54.00 back on 5 spots with 2 taken); a paid-for person the business removes sends that spot's price back to the buyer at any time; and each unsent refund is remembered on its own (two failed, both were sent a moment later, once each). All five gaps are closed on `dev`, each seen by its session in test mode to the cent with the payout after it; the coordinator read both changes. Not checked: the payment company's call-back, live mode.

Android's is on `dev` ([#4372](https://github.com/WangPantopus/skinny-pantopus/pull/4372), November 17; the coordinator read it before it merged: from the first moment a spot link opens the page's footer is the spot's, and only the server's "not available" or a closed offer brings back the ordinary page): its session saw on an emulator a live link with "QA got you a spot" and "I'm in" in the footer and no "Join"; with the backend stopped, "I'm in" and then "Try again" each reading "Couldn't check your spot. Try again."; with it back, the offer live again and the spot taken, free; a stopped link reading its closed words with the ordinary "Join"; and with the switch off today's page. The iPhone's claim, with the same rule, is on `dev` as well ([#4366](https://github.com/WangPantopus/skinny-pantopus/pull/4366), November 17; the coordinator read it before it merged and sent one fault back, fixed in it): its session saw on two simulators in test mode one account buy 3 spots by link ($54.00) and the other open the spot link in the app, read "Sam got you a spot" with "I'm in" and no "Join", and be in, paid by the buyer, with no payment of its own, also after a relaunch; a person already in read "You're already on this plan."; a stopped link read "This link isn't taking anyone now." and then the ordinary "Join"; with the backend stopped while the offer was read, "Couldn't check your spot. Try again." and then the offer; stopped during the claim, the offer kept and then taken; with the switch off the ordinary page. One small tightening followed ([#4376](https://github.com/WangPantopus/skinny-pantopus/pull/4376), on `dev`): an answer of "too many requests" or a timeout counts as "couldn't check", not as a clear answer; its session made the test server answer "too many requests" to the offer's read and to the claim, and each time the phone kept the spot and showed "Try again" with no "Join", then took the spot, with no payment of its own. Not seen yet on the iPhone: the link through the browser page's "Open app". The browser page a buyer's link opens also dropped the spot from its "Open app" button; fixed on `dev` ([#4367](https://github.com/WangPantopus/skinny-pantopus/pull/4367), web only, read by the coordinator): its session saw in a browser, in test mode, a live link reading "QA got you a spot" with "I'm in" and no pay panel, both app links carrying the spot, a stopped link reading "This link isn't taking anyone now." and then the ordinary "Join for $18", and with the switch off today's page. (The web page can also take the spot itself after sign-in; that was already built.) The web page had the same weakness as the phones when the offer could not be read; fixed on `dev` ([#4370](https://github.com/WangPantopus/skinny-pantopus/pull/4370), web only, read by the coordinator): its session saw in a browser a live link with the offer and no "Join"; the offer's read made to time out, and the backend stopped during a claim, each reading "Couldn't check your spot. Try again." with no pay panel and the spot kept; "Try again" after the backend came back showing the offer; a stopped link reading its closed words and then "Join for $18"; and with the switch off today's page. **Until then, `plans_group_pay` stays off wherever people have an iPhone build without it** (it is off everywhere today). **Run again after the iPhone's claim merged, and passed** (both apps and the backend from `dev` 1fda6b7a42, about 9:20 AM Pacific on November 17): the buyer on Android and the claimer on the iPhone, where the spot link opened into the iPhone app showed "QA got you a spot" with a free "I'm in" in place of "Join", and the iPhone was in with no payment of its own; the buyer on the iPhone and the claimer on Android, where Android's footer was only "I'm in", and the iPhone buyer's give-back returned $18.00; a stopped link on the iPhone read "This link isn't taking anyone now." and then showed the ordinary page. With the group-pay switch off and a purchase in place, the buyer's card was gone and the leave sentence read "You'll get $54.00 back: your spot and the 2 nobody took. The 1 person you paid for stays in.", which is what happened: one $54.00 refund, the other person still in. Find a date's two lines were seen on both phones: an invited account read "You're in when QA picks a time you ticked." and one that came by the open link with "I say yes first" on read "When QA picks a time you ticked, QA lets you in." Then the spot link the way a person gets it, through the browser page (same builds, the web from the same `dev`). On the iPhone: Safari showed "QA got you a spot" with "I'm in" and no pay panel; "Open app" asked "Open in Pantopus?" and opened the app on the offer with the free "I'm in" and no "Join"; the spot was taken with no payment. In a desktop browser, signed in as another test account: "I'm in" on the page itself, then "You're in", no payment; a stopped link read "This link isn't taking anyone now." with "Join for $18". **On Android this found one more way to the paid "Join", now being fixed:** the page's "Open app" hands the app the plan's own address with the spot attached, and Android read the spot only from the link's address, so through "Open app" an Android user landed on the ordinary paid "Join" (the Android step was run by opening that address directly; the emulator's browser stopped at a first-run screen). Opening the link itself on Android goes straight into the app and was right. Android now reads the spot from both ([#4388](https://github.com/WangPantopus/skinny-pantopus/pull/4388), on `dev`, November 17, the last change of this build; read by the coordinator, a few lines in how Android reads a link): its session saw on an emulator the plan's own address with a spot attached open on "QA got you a spot" with the free "I'm in", and the spot taken with no payment by the person taking it; a stopped link reading "This link isn't taking anyone now." with the ordinary "Join"; the same address without a spot showing today's page; and the link's own address still working. Not seen: the button itself tapped in a phone's browser on Android (the address it carries was opened directly), and the web address form on a store build. Not covered: the payment company's call-back, live mode, Apple Pay and Google Pay.
- **Only you** (the whole list in one place; the building is finished and none of this was waited for):
  - **Look and say what you want changed.** Boards 24 to 31, the extras, which you had not seen when they were built. And the calls the designer made while it was built, each marked "settled" or "decided" in [08-extras.md](docs/product/plans-holidays/08-extras.md), [07-screens-business.md](docs/product/plans-holidays/07-screens-business.md) and [the contract](docs/product/plans-holidays/03-build-contract.md): the sentences ("Paid $18.00 · $4.00 back", "Nobody new was invited.", "When {Host} picks a time you ticked, {Host} lets you in.", "Couldn't check your spot. Try again."), that an invitation names no date while a date is being picked, that a guest without an account who doesn't fit becomes a Maybe, that the host may join the gift exchange, that "{7} of {12} paid" counts answers, and that a group purchase settles the same whatever its switch says. A change of words is one small server change; the apps draw what the server sends.
  - **Three things that are not built and wait for your word:** a named group kept between plans (it needs a new table); whether starting from the "Gift exchange" occasion should switch the gift exchange on by itself (today the host switches it on in the form); and letting a host move a posted plan to "My place" from the Change screen (the server already holds guests for the host's yes when a plan moves to a home; neither app offers the move for a plan posted at a public place). Also not built, by the design's own decision: "Two for one" as a deal, and a screen for the business statement.
  - **On staging.** Your check of guests answering in a browser (it is on there since November 15; the steps are above). Anything else needs a staging build of each phone from `dev` and its switch turned on in staging's database (the line above gives the statement; the switches are `plans_bring_list`, `plans_occasions`, `plans_deals`, `plans_paid`, `plans_pay_here`, `plans_same_people`, `plans_chip_in`, `plans_find_date`, `plans_gift_exchange`, `plans_photos`, `plans_group_pay`). Staging's web site may lag: its host refused new deployments on November 17 for having too many that day.
  - **Before any paid step is switched on for real people** ([build plan, section 3](docs/product/plans-holidays/04-build-plan.md#3-the-business-track-and-its-gates)): the by-hand trial with two or three local businesses; the lawyer's read of the terms; a re-read of both stores' payment rules; the accountant's answer on Washington sales tax; the one-minute look at "Fee" in the live Stripe dashboard (above); whether a card statement can name the business; and one real payment end to end on staging, with the payment company's call-back, Apple Pay and Google Pay on a real phone. None of those was or could be checked by a session.
  - **Before photos is switched on anywhere:** that environment needs private storage and the picture check's key; then add one picture and see the real check pass it. Every check so far used a stand-in.
  - **Switches that depend on a phone build.** An app tells the server it can draw the extras from the first extras build on, so: `plans_find_date` only where people's phone builds have the find-a-date screens; `plans_group_pay` only where they have taking a spot from the link with "a bought spot is never paid for twice" (iPhone #4366 and #4376, Android #4372 and #4388). A store build made from `dev` as it stands on November 17 after #4388 has all of it.
  - **The promotion of `dev` to master**, then each switch in production, then the iPhone and Android store builds. The promotion carries, beside the new code behind its switches: one added step in account deletion (it removes a person's plan photos; with none anywhere it does nothing), the refund fix in the shared refund code (plan payments only; gigs and invoices are reconciled as before), and additive database changes (added columns, no new table).
  - **What no session could check:** a physical phone; a real push arriving on an iPhone or on Android; hosted email delivery; Safari on a real iPhone; VoiceOver and TalkBack speaking aloud; Apple Pay and Google Pay; the payment company's call-back; live money; the real picture check and hosted private storage; staging beyond its health answer; the canvas page itself.
  - **Seen in passing, outside this build:** the Pantopus AI sheet says "never shared with neighbors"; Android's notice list needs a pull to show a new notice; the test setups' day limits (10 private plans a person, 20 plans a business) got in the way of a few checks, which is said where it happened.

## Plans: private, repeating, online and four new kinds (added November 15)

Decided November 14 (section 0). Drawn first ([the boards](https://claude.ai/artifact/3FXiTKmK5gvTFBid2StVSk)), then built November 14 and 15 by one coordinating session and one session each for the iPhone and Android. Checked in the iPhone simulator, the Android emulator and a browser against a local backend and database, with test accounts acting on each other's plans across the two phones.

- [x] Server and web share pages, on `dev` and staging: private plans ([#4103](https://github.com/WangPantopus/skinny-pantopus/pull/4103)), repeating plans ([#4105](https://github.com/WangPantopus/skinny-pantopus/pull/4105)), online plans and the four kinds ([#4111](https://github.com/WangPantopus/skinny-pantopus/pull/4111)). Three additive migrations, no new table. Four switches, all on: `plans_private`, `plans_repeat`, `plans_online`, `plans_kinds`.
- [x] iPhone: [#4104](https://github.com/WangPantopus/skinny-pantopus/pull/4104) (words and private plans), [#4129](https://github.com/WangPantopus/skinny-pantopus/pull/4129) (repeating), [#4130](https://github.com/WangPantopus/skinny-pantopus/pull/4130) (online and the kinds), [#4175](https://github.com/WangPantopus/skinny-pantopus/pull/4175) (the end date). Android: [#4126](https://github.com/WangPantopus/skinny-pantopus/pull/4126), [#4131](https://github.com/WangPantopus/skinny-pantopus/pull/4131), [#4132](https://github.com/WangPantopus/skinny-pantopus/pull/4132), [#4176](https://github.com/WangPantopus/skinny-pantopus/pull/4176).
- [x] Found by the phone checks and fixed on the server: changing how a plan repeats on a later date of its series now starts the new pattern at that date and keeps the count true ("3 of 7"); a private plan was listed twice for its host; a private plan posted right after a public one with the same name came back as the public one ([#4127](https://github.com/WangPantopus/skinny-pantopus/pull/4127)); an online plan's reminder showed "On my way" ([#4162](https://github.com/WangPantopus/skinny-pantopus/pull/4162)).
- [x] Your end-date check (November 15): on both phones a date in 2027 picks and saves, past days are greyed, and nothing more than a year out can be picked. One hole: the plan's own date could be the end, which posted a "repeating" plan with one date. The server now refuses it ("It has to end on or after its second date, Sunday, November 22.", [#4173](https://github.com/WangPantopus/skinny-pantopus/pull/4173)) and both pickers start at the second date. "After a number of times" now starts at the number the end date gives (it started at 6 on the iPhone and 13 on Android). On the iPhone the calendar and its month and year wheels scroll into view; they used to open half under the Done button.
- [x] What the two phones did together, on builds of `dev`: a private, weekly, online game night posted on the iPhone showed on Android for the invited guest only, who said "I'm in" and got the link, and the host's count changed; "Skip Sunday" on the iPhone posted the next date and told the guest; changing the ending from 6 times to a date carried to the server and to Android. A public Outdoors plan every 2 weeks posted on Android showed on the iPhone with its pin and its repeat line; the iPhone asked, Android accepted, the iPhone showed "You're in" with the exact place. When a date ended, the next one was posted by itself and the regular was invited. Both apps kept everything after a relaunch. The public plan's web page shows its kind and repeat line; a private plan's page says only "This plan is private".
- Known limits:
  - Store builds from before this can't draw an online plan or a new kind. They don't list them, but they still get the notices (an invitation, "it is posted") and open a plan that isn't there for them, until the stores carry these steps.
  - A person's "plans in common" on a profile leaves out online plans and the new kinds.
  - Who can see a plan can't change after it is posted. A plain Cancel on a repeating plan stops the series (the phones ask "Cancel this date" or "Cancel and stop repeating").
  - After a series is stopped, an ended earlier date doesn't offer "Host again"; the last date stays posted and can be set to repeat again.
  - Pantopus AI still drafts only the five older kinds.
  - A private plan more than a week out shows under "Your plans", not in the "This week" list.
- Small things seen, not changed: the type picker's one-line descriptions of the five older kinds differ between the iPhone and Android (as before this work); hosting as a business says "five kinds" over four cards.
- [ ] Not checked anywhere: the hosted staging site with a staging phone build, real phones, real push and email delivery, a screen reader speaking aloud, large text sizes, the iPhone Duo layout.
- [ ] Not built: finding a time together and keeping a group between plans. It needs a new table, so it needs your approval first.

## Guidance cards for new accounts (added November 17)

Decided November 16 (section 0). Drawn first ([the design page](https://claude.ai/artifact/DtfFKNGLUZqqzQhmfKHk2s)), then built November 17 by one coordinating session and one session each for the server, the web, the iPhone and Android. Each app was checked against a local backend and database: the web in a browser, the iPhone in the simulator, Android in the emulator.

- [x] Server ([#4315](https://github.com/WangPantopus/skinny-pantopus/pull/4315), into `dev`):
  - one new field on the users table, `User.guidance_seen`: which cards a person has closed, how many times and when;
  - the profile answers `guidance` (switch on or off, whether the account is under 14 days old, and what it has seen);
  - `POST /api/users/guidance/shown` records a closed card, and `/reset` clears them for testing;
  - one switch, `GUIDANCE_CARDS`;
  - no new table.
- [x] Web: cards 1–5, 8–15 and Tips ([#4320](https://github.com/WangPantopus/skinny-pantopus/pull/4320)), with the seven old pop-ups removed; cards 16–28 ([#4343](https://github.com/WangPantopus/skinny-pantopus/pull/4343)). Moments and Plans cards are phone-only, because the web has neither tab.
- [x] iPhone: cards 1–15 and Tips ([#4325](https://github.com/WangPantopus/skinny-pantopus/pull/4325)); cards 16–28 ([#4377](https://github.com/WangPantopus/skinny-pantopus/pull/4377)).
- [x] Android: cards 1–15 and Tips ([#4340](https://github.com/WangPantopus/skinny-pantopus/pull/4340)); the status bar's clock and icons stay visible over a card ([#4374](https://github.com/WangPantopus/skinny-pantopus/pull/4374)); cards 16–28 ([#4381](https://github.com/WangPantopus/skinny-pantopus/pull/4381)).
- [x] When a card may show, on every app:
  - only while the server's switch is on, and only on a person's own account (never a business);
  - one card at a time, half a second after the screen has loaded;
  - never over the daily briefings sheet, the phone's own questions, Face ID, a link that is opening, a sheet, the keyboard or the side menu;
  - a tab's card the first time that tab opens; any other card at most one per app open;
  - the pointers from the second app open;
  - the reminders on Place only, at most one a week, never on a day another card showed, and at most twice each.
- [x] A closed card is kept on the phone at once and sent to the server, and the server's record wins when the app reads the profile. So a card closed on one app doesn't show on another.
- [x] The words are the same on all three apps. The only differences are where a control differs:
  - the web on a computer has no tabs, so card 1 says "The list on the left opens each part of the app.";
  - the web's buttons say “Set up this home” and “My posts”;
  - the web's Mail writes a letter from its ••• menu.
- [x] What the three-app check showed (November 17, one brand-new account on the web, the iPhone simulator and the Android emulator, each built from `dev`, against a local backend and database):
  - **Web:** the daily briefings notice came first with no card over it, then card 1 with your two lines; "Get started" recorded it on the server.
  - **Android:** card 1 didn't come back. Today's card showed on Today's first open, with the clock and status icons visible over it, and the X closed it.
  - **iPhone:** neither card 1 nor Today's card came back. Pulse's card showed in dark mode, and a tap outside closed it.
  - **Web again:** no card on Today or Pulse.
  - **Android after a relaunch:** the "Messages" pointer showed (the second app open), "Got it" closed it, and Pulse showed nothing.
  - **iPhone after a relaunch:** it skipped "Messages", already closed on Android, and showed the next pointer, "Menu", in dark mode.
  - **The server's record:** five cards, each closed once.

  Each app's own checks (every card, the rules, large text, screen-reader order) are in its PR.
- Seen only through the test switch, because the local rig can't reach them: "It's on its way" (needs a real letter sent); "Introducing Plans" (none is switched on); on some apps, "Your household" and the verify reminder.
- [ ] Not checked anywhere: the hosted staging site with a staging phone build, real phones, a screen reader speaking aloud (the reading order was checked), and the iPhone Duo.
- [ ] Yours:
  - staging has all of it once the phones' staging builds carry it;
  - it reaches production when you promote `dev` to `master`;
  - production shows nothing until you set `GUIDANCE_CARDS=1` on the production API (see [the production checklist](docs/release/prod-config-checklist.md));
  - the store apps show it once new store builds carry it.

## Christmas cards: easier to read, answer and send (added November 17)

You asked on November 17 what else could be better in sending, drafting, receiving and opening a card, then said "Plan everything" and answered the plan's decisions the same morning: [the plan](docs/product/mail-cards-experience-2026-11-17/README.md), with your answers in its section "Decided by the founder". Three Opus 5.5 sessions built it the same day: the server and web, the iPhone with the Duo, and Android. Each part was checked in the real apps against a local backend and database with made-up people, from both people's side and after a reload or relaunch. Both hand-made moments, opening the envelope by hand and sealing it by hand, are unchanged.

All ten parts are on `dev` for all three apps:

- [x] Part 1, the pen fits the note: the pen is 30, 27 or 23 pt on an iPhone 17 (9, 10 or 11 ruled lines), the writing box steps the same way as you type, and the share pictures follow. Web [#4387](https://github.com/WangPantopus/skinny-pantopus/pull/4387), iPhone [#4395](https://github.com/WangPantopus/skinny-pantopus/pull/4395), Android [#4383](https://github.com/WangPantopus/skinny-pantopus/pull/4383).
- [x] Part 2, Read it in print: the note in plain type on the verse's bookmark, growing with the phone's text size. Web [#4390](https://github.com/WangPantopus/skinny-pantopus/pull/4390), iPhone [#4396](https://github.com/WangPantopus/skinny-pantopus/pull/4396), Android [#4384](https://github.com/WangPantopus/skinny-pantopus/pull/4384).
- [x] Part 8, small fixes: "Verified resident" under a verified sender on a card's link page, and a larger hint under the table's button. On the web the card table also can't scroll inside itself. Web [#4391](https://github.com/WangPantopus/skinny-pantopus/pull/4391), iPhone [#4404](https://github.com/WangPantopus/skinny-pantopus/pull/4404), Android [#4385](https://github.com/WangPantopus/skinny-pantopus/pull/4385).
- [x] Part 5, shorter sending (your D5 answer): "Seal and send" above "Look it over" on "Seal it for the day", straight to the sealing table. Look it over keeps only its button at the bottom, so the card shows down to the button. Web [#4397](https://github.com/WangPantopus/skinny-pantopus/pull/4397), iPhone [#4398](https://github.com/WangPantopus/skinny-pantopus/pull/4398), Android [#4386](https://github.com/WangPantopus/skinny-pantopus/pull/4386).
- [x] Part 4, Open the next card: from one waiting card to the next without going back to the list. Web [#4399](https://github.com/WangPantopus/skinny-pantopus/pull/4399), iPhone [#4405](https://github.com/WangPantopus/skinny-pantopus/pull/4405), Android [#4389](https://github.com/WangPantopus/skinny-pantopus/pull/4389).
- [x] Part 6, Send this card to someone else: the same picture and signature, a new note, three pages. Web [#4400](https://github.com/WangPantopus/skinny-pantopus/pull/4400), iPhone [#4406](https://github.com/WangPantopus/skinny-pantopus/pull/4406), Android [#4392](https://github.com/WangPantopus/skinny-pantopus/pull/4392).
- [x] Part 3, Write back (the reply approved on November 2), note only. Server [#4394](https://github.com/WangPantopus/skinny-pantopus/pull/4394): one reply per card, free, with the other person's next 7 a.m. mail, can be taken back until pickup, withdrawn by a block. It sits behind one new server-only switch, `card_replies`. No migration and no new table. Web [#4402](https://github.com/WangPantopus/skinny-pantopus/pull/4402), iPhone [#4412](https://github.com/WangPantopus/skinny-pantopus/pull/4412), Android [#4430](https://github.com/WangPantopus/skinny-pantopus/pull/4430). On all three the reply sits below the card's picture, so the whole card shows, as you asked on November 17 (on the web, [#4408](https://github.com/WangPantopus/skinny-pantopus/pull/4408)). A reply stays off the mantel and out of "Open the next card".
- [x] Part 7, the mantel, from the sample you approved: web [#4407](https://github.com/WangPantopus/skinny-pantopus/pull/4407), iPhone [#4409](https://github.com/WangPantopus/skinny-pantopus/pull/4409), Android [#4393](https://github.com/WangPantopus/skinny-pantopus/pull/4393). Each card leans the same way on all three apps (Android [#4416](https://github.com/WangPantopus/skinny-pantopus/pull/4416)).
- [x] The design check you asked for on November 17: the built parts matched the plan apart from three layout problems, fixed on all three apps the same evening. Under a turned-over card sits one row of at most two quiet buttons, never stacked, so the card keeps its size on small phones and at the largest text size (web [#4453](https://github.com/WangPantopus/skinny-pantopus/pull/4453) and [#4456](https://github.com/WangPantopus/skinny-pantopus/pull/4456), iPhone [#4458](https://github.com/WangPantopus/skinny-pantopus/pull/4458), Android [#4463](https://github.com/WangPantopus/skinny-pantopus/pull/4463)). "Seal and send" and "Look it over" both show the moment "Seal it for the day" opens, with solid cloth behind "Look it over" and a refusal on its own cream slip above them (web [#4454](https://github.com/WangPantopus/skinny-pantopus/pull/4454) and [#4460](https://github.com/WangPantopus/skinny-pantopus/pull/4460), iPhone [#4459](https://github.com/WangPantopus/skinny-pantopus/pull/4459), Android [#4464](https://github.com/WangPantopus/skinny-pantopus/pull/4464)). On the web a card joined to its reply, turned note side up, has the reply lying lower so the card's FROM line stays clear (web [#4455](https://github.com/WangPantopus/skinny-pantopus/pull/4455)); on the phones that card never turns, so nothing changed there. The plan's Parts 3, 4 and 5 now say the same.
- [x] Part 9, a long note runs onto a second leaf (added the evening of November 17, when you asked how a long note could keep showing everything without shortening what "Help me write it" writes): a note longer than the middle pen's 10 lines gets more paper instead of smaller writing. It is written over two leaves in the largest pen that holds it there: the first leaf lies on the card's back with "over" at its foot, and "Read on" lifts it to show the rest, FROM and the signature. A word is never split between the leaves, and a closing line after a blank line, such as "Merry Christmas!", may stand alone on the second leaf. The writing step says "This note runs onto a second leaf." Web [#4474](https://github.com/WangPantopus/skinny-pantopus/pull/4474) and [#4479](https://github.com/WangPantopus/skinny-pantopus/pull/4479), iPhone and Duo [#4478](https://github.com/WangPantopus/skinny-pantopus/pull/4478), Android [#4487](https://github.com/WangPantopus/skinny-pantopus/pull/4487).
- [x] Part 10, "Help me write it" names one person only, as you asked on November 17: a note for two or more people names no one ("Dear friends," or straight into the note) and doesn't say how many people get it; one person is still greeted by first name. Server and web [#4472](https://github.com/WangPantopus/skinny-pantopus/pull/4472), iPhone [#4475](https://github.com/WangPantopus/skinny-pantopus/pull/4475), Android [#4476](https://github.com/WangPantopus/skinny-pantopus/pull/4476). The server change went live on staging at 10:27 p.m. Pacific on November 17; the help is on in production, so production writes the new way once you promote `dev`.

Also checked:

- [x] A store build that knows cards but not replies (iPhone tag `ios-v2.1.3`) shows a reply as a plain letter, "A reply to a Christmas card", with its note, and so does an Android build from before replies. So replies don't break today's store apps.

Yours:

- [x] The reply's server change ([#4394](https://github.com/WangPantopus/skinny-pantopus/pull/4394)) went live on staging at 12:22 p.m. Pacific on November 17.

Building started November 17 from one written contract: one session each for the server, the web, the iPhone and Android on Opus 5.5 at Extra High, and a final check of everything on Fable 5.2 when they finish. Each line is ticked when it is on `dev` for every app it touches and checked in the real apps.

- [ ] Base: the three levels on the server, and the apps' existing verify sheet reading its choices from the server.

Where it stands (November 17, about 4 PM Pacific):

- Server: finished. Every phase is merged into `dev`, each switched off ([#4413](https://github.com/WangPantopus/skinny-pantopus/pull/4413), [#4415](https://github.com/WangPantopus/skinny-pantopus/pull/4415), [#4418](https://github.com/WangPantopus/skinny-pantopus/pull/4418), [#4420](https://github.com/WangPantopus/skinny-pantopus/pull/4420), [#4422](https://github.com/WangPantopus/skinny-pantopus/pull/4422), [#4424](https://github.com/WangPantopus/skinny-pantopus/pull/4424)). The final check reviewed it, fixed what it found ([#4442](https://github.com/WangPantopus/skinny-pantopus/pull/4442)) and has no findings left.
- Web: finished. Everything is merged ([#4417](https://github.com/WangPantopus/skinny-pantopus/pull/4417), [#4419](https://github.com/WangPantopus/skinny-pantopus/pull/4419), [#4421](https://github.com/WangPantopus/skinny-pantopus/pull/4421), [#4423](https://github.com/WangPantopus/skinny-pantopus/pull/4423), [#4425](https://github.com/WangPantopus/skinny-pantopus/pull/4425), [#4433](https://github.com/WangPantopus/skinny-pantopus/pull/4433), [#4435](https://github.com/WangPantopus/skinny-pantopus/pull/4435), [#4440](https://github.com/WangPantopus/skinny-pantopus/pull/4440), [#4446](https://github.com/WangPantopus/skinny-pantopus/pull/4446)). The final check reviewed it in two rounds and has no findings left.
- iPhone: all of it is in seven open PRs (#4439, #4441, #4447, #4448, #4449, #4450, #4451), handed to the final check and not merged. The check has read the code; using the app waits for a free simulator.
- Android: all of it is in six open PRs (#4414, #4427, #4428, #4436, #4437, #4438), not merged. A last pass in the emulator comes before they are handed to the final check.
- Your rule from about 2 PM: the builders no longer merge. Each uses everything it built in the real app, then hands its open PRs to the final check on Fable 5.2, which reviews them and merges the ones it finds clean.
- At about 4 PM you asked the iPhone and Android builders, whose sessions were full, to hand over to new sessions on Fable 5.2 that reuse the same test setups. Both successors are running.

Found while planning:

- The apps already had a verify sheet listing mail, a document and the landlord. The fast checks are added to it; no second screen is built.
- A location reading can be faked by sending a home's coordinates to the server. The location check therefore needs Apple's App Attest and Google's Play Integrity, is built last, and stays off in production until real phones have passed it.
- A fast check is kept as a mark on the claimed home, so every claim rule already built still applies: the address watch runs first, homes with a household are left out, and proof by mail outranks it.

What the builders can't check: real texts and phone-company records, the device proofs (real phones and store-signed builds), real mail and scanning a printed card, and production.

## December card push (added November 24)

Approved by you on November 24: every account starts with seven free stamps instead of three ("we can do 7 instead of 10"), and the Christmas card loop is counted. Server and web only, no new table, no switch.

- [ ] **Seven free stamps** ([#4774](https://github.com/WangPantopus/skinny-pantopus/pull/4774)). A new account starts with 7 free stamps. An account made before gets 4 more the first time its stamps are read, so its Stamps history shows "Your free cards +3" and then "+4". `/cards`, `/mail`, the homepage and the guides say 7. A card's own photo or voice still needs a bought stamp. Sending closes by itself when December 31 ends.
- [ ] **Card events** ([#4776](https://github.com/WangPantopus/skinny-pantopus/pull/4776)). The server records seven card events: sent, taken back, link page seen, code sent, claimed, written back and passed on. They are counts only, with no names, addresses, notes or ids, and sending, opening and claiming are no slower.
- [ ] **The card report** ([#4778](https://github.com/WangPantopus/skinny-pantopus/pull/4778)). It is at `/app/admin/funnel` on the web (admins only): the cards by UTC day with the window's totals on top (senders, cards sent and how, taken back, link pages seen, codes, claimed, joined, joiners who sent their own within 7 days, replies, pass-ons, stamp books sold by size, and the loop number, which is joiners who sent their own per sender), then the ladder, Plans and first-week numbers the summary already had. "Pages seen" is a ceiling, not people. Counting starts when this reaches production; nothing before it is recorded.
- [ ] **Yours: promote `dev` to `master`** (one merge-commit PR, as usual). This applies the two migrations to production (`20261124120000_seven_free_stamps` and `20261124121000_card_funnel_events`). Nothing to switch on.
- [ ] Not checked: the iPhone and Android apps as released (they print the server's number and list both free rows as "Your free cards", from reading their code), anything on staging or production, and real email delivery.

## Around you: the everywhere layer (added November 24)

You asked on November 24 what people in a place with few users (Miami; Leavenworth, WA) would see on Around you, and chose "the everywhere layer": public feeds for any US address, a quiet-day card, page rules, Clark County source repairs, and a source finder so a new county gets its own sources. Server and web admin only; no app change, no store build.

- [ ] **Sources for a new county** (piece 4, `around-sources`). When the first home or location is set in a new place, the hourly feeds job hands that region, once, to the source finder (at most 20 places a day). The finder proposes the state's capitol outlet from a fixed table of all 50 states (States Newsroom's own list, every feed tested), then asks Claude, with web search, for the county's and the city's own sources: county and city news pages, the school district, the power utility, local papers, radio, television and nonprofit newsrooms, and the chamber's, library's and city's calendars. The server then reads every address itself and counts what it holds: items, items in the last 7 days and the last 2 days, how many name the city or county, three sample titles, and whether a story page asks for a subscription. Nothing it finds shows to anyone until an admin approves it on `/app/admin/sources` (web), where each place has "Find sources", the proposals with their numbers, Approve (name on cards, free or subscription, how long stories stay) or Reject, the addresses it checked and did not propose with the reason, and "Add by hand". An approved feed is read from the content fetcher's next run, an approved calendar from the feeds job's next run.
  - Every address the model gives is treated as hostile: it is read only over https, only from a public address (private, loopback, link-local and cloud metadata addresses are refused, checked on the connection itself and again on every redirect), with at most 3 redirects within the same site, 20 seconds and 5 MB. A site whose robots.txt or answer refuses automated readers is noted and not proposed. Google News, Facebook and Reddit are never proposed. The request to Claude carries the city, county and state names only.
  - What a check on a local backend and database showed (November 24, real Claude runs): for Leavenworth, the chamber's calendar (30 events, 27 naming Leavenworth), KPQ radio (35 items, 2 local), the city's own news feed, and The Wenatchee World's feed shown as not local (10 items, none naming Leavenworth or Chelan County: syndicated product stories); the Leavenworth Echo answered 403 to an automated reader and was noted, not proposed. For Miami: Miami Today (every item names Miami), Miami New Times (7 of 10), FPL's newsroom, and The Miami Times' feed (0 of 10: syndicated press releases); the Miami Herald gave no answer within 20 seconds. Approving the chamber's calendar put five Leavenworth events on Around you; approving Miami New Times put its stories at the top of Miami's Headlines, on the web, as posts.
  - Cost: each run used 21k to 36k input tokens (most of them cache reads), 1.5k to 1.8k output tokens and 1 to 8 web searches on Claude Opus 5.5: about $0.10 to $0.20 a run.
  - A weekly paper's stories reach Around you only in the two days after each issue, because the content fetcher keeps a story for 48 hours after it was published; the review page shows that count.
  - When the Claude step gives no answer (no key, no credit, an error, or out of its budget), the place is not used up: the feeds job hands it to the finder again the next hour (twice at most after a run that spent tokens). The line under a proposal that the model wrote is marked "Claude's note"; the numbers and sample titles are the server's own.
  - The review page shows a run's summary (Claude's outcome, tokens, counts) only for a run made in the same server process, such as one started with "Find sources"; a run the worker's hourly job made shows only "Checked <time>" and its rows.
- [ ] **Yours: production.** Set `AROUND_YOU_SOURCE_FINDER=1` on the production server if new counties should get proposals there, and make sure the backend's Anthropic key has credit ([the production checklist](docs/release/prod-config-checklist.md)). Unset, it is off in production and on in staging. Nothing shows until someone approves a proposal on `/app/admin/sources`.
- Not checked: the hosted staging and production sites, staging's Anthropic key, the seeder Lambda in AWS (its fetcher was run by hand on the local database), and the phones (no phone screen changed). The seeder's own feed reader does not check for private addresses; an approved feed is read by it as any hand-added feed is. Giving the seeder the same guard needs a change under `pantopus-seeder/` and your `sam deploy`.
